<p><b>Mongolia</b><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="/ŋ/: &#39;ng&#39; in &#39;sing&#39;">ŋ</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/ɡ/: &#39;g&#39; in &#39;guy&#39;">ɡ</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Mongolia.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/82/En-us-Mongolia.ogg" class="internal" title="En-us-Mongolia.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span></span>) is a <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked country</a> in <a href="/wiki/East_Asia" title="East Asia">East Asia</a>, bordered by <a href="/wiki/Russia" title="Russia">Russia</a> <a href="/wiki/Mongolia%E2%80%93Russia_border" title="Mongolia–Russia border">to the north</a> and <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a> <a href="/wiki/China%E2%80%93Mongolia_border" title="China–Mongolia border">to the south</a>. It covers an area of 1,564,116 square kilometres (603,909 square miles), with a population of just 3.3 million, making it the world's <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population_density" title="List of countries and dependencies by population density">most sparsely populated sovereign nation</a>. Mongolia is the world's largest landlocked country that does not border a <a href="/wiki/Endorheic_basin" title="Endorheic basin">closed sea</a>, and much of its area is covered by grassy <a href="/wiki/Steppe" title="Steppe">steppe</a>, with <a href="/wiki/Mountain" title="Mountain">mountains</a> to the north and west and the <a href="/wiki/Gobi_Desert" title="Gobi Desert">Gobi Desert</a> to the south. <a href="/wiki/Ulaanbaatar" title="Ulaanbaatar">Ulaanbaatar</a>, the capital and <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Mongolia" title="List of cities in Mongolia">largest city</a>, is home to roughly half of the country's population.
</p><p>The territory of modern-day Mongolia has been ruled by various <a href="/wiki/Nomadic_empire" title="Nomadic empire">nomadic empires</a>, including the <a href="/wiki/Xiongnu" title="Xiongnu">Xiongnu</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Xianbei" title="Xianbei">Xianbei</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Rouran" class="mw-redirect" title="Rouran">Rouran</a>, the <a href="/wiki/First_Turkic_Khaganate" title="First Turkic Khaganate">First Turkic Khaganate</a>, and others. In 1206, <a href="/wiki/Genghis_Khan" title="Genghis Khan">Genghis Khan</a> founded the <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol Empire</a>, which became the largest <a href="/wiki/List_of_largest_empires" title="List of largest empires">contiguous land empire</a> in history. His grandson <a href="/wiki/Kublai_Khan" title="Kublai Khan">Kublai Khan</a> conquered <a href="/wiki/China_proper" title="China proper">China proper</a> and established the <a href="/wiki/Yuan_dynasty" title="Yuan dynasty">Yuan dynasty</a>. After the collapse of the Yuan, the <a href="/wiki/Mongols" title="Mongols">Mongols</a> retreated to Mongolia and resumed their earlier pattern of factional conflict, except during the era of <a href="/wiki/Dayan_Khan" title="Dayan Khan">Dayan Khan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tumen_Zasagt_Khan" class="mw-redirect" title="Tumen Zasagt Khan">Tumen Zasagt Khan</a>. In the 16th century, <a href="/wiki/Buddhism_in_Mongolia" title="Buddhism in Mongolia">Tibetan Buddhism</a> spread to Mongolia, being further led by the <a href="/wiki/Manchu_people" title="Manchu people">Manchu</a>-founded <a href="/wiki/Qing_dynasty" title="Qing dynasty">Qing dynasty</a>, which absorbed the country in the 17th century. By the early 20th century, almost one-third of the adult male population were Buddhist monks.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> After the collapse of the Qing dynasty in 1911, Mongolia <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Revolution_of_1911" title="Mongolian Revolution of 1911">declared independence</a>, and achieved actual <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Revolution_of_1921" title="Mongolian Revolution of 1921">independence from the Republic of China</a> in 1921. Shortly thereafter, the country became a <a href="/wiki/Satellite_state" title="Satellite state">satellite state</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>, which had aided its independence from China. In 1924, the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Republic" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Republic">Mongolian People's Republic</a> was founded as a <a href="/wiki/Socialist_state" title="Socialist state">socialist state</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sik_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sik-15">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> After the anti-communist <a href="/wiki/Revolutions_of_1989" title="Revolutions of 1989">revolutions of 1989</a>, Mongolia conducted its own peaceful <a href="/wiki/1990_Democratic_Revolution_in_Mongolia" class="mw-redirect" title="1990 Democratic Revolution in Mongolia">democratic revolution</a> in early 1990. This led to a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a>, a new <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Mongolia" title="Constitution of Mongolia">constitution of 1992</a>, and transition to a <a href="/wiki/Market_economy" title="Market economy">market economy</a>.
</p><p>Approximately 30% of the population is <a href="/wiki/Nomad" title="Nomad">nomadic</a> or semi-nomadic; <a href="/wiki/Horse_culture_in_Mongolia" title="Horse culture in Mongolia">horse culture</a> remains integral. <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhism</a> is the majority religion (51.7%), with the nonreligious being the second-largest group (40.6%). <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> is the third-largest religious identification (3.2%), concentrated among ethnic <a href="/wiki/Kazakhs" title="Kazakhs">Kazakhs</a>. The vast majority of citizens are ethnic Mongols, with roughly 5% of the population being <a href="/wiki/Kazakhs" title="Kazakhs">Kazakhs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tuvans" title="Tuvans">Tuvans</a>, and other ethnic minorities, who are especially concentrated in the west. Mongolia is a member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, <a href="/wiki/Asia_Cooperation_Dialogue" title="Asia Cooperation Dialogue">Asia Cooperation Dialogue</a>, <a href="/wiki/Group_of_77" title="Group of 77">G77</a>, <a href="/wiki/Asian_Infrastructure_Investment_Bank" title="Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank">Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a> and a <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a> <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_NATO" title="Foreign relations of NATO">global partner</a>. Mongolia joined the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a> in 1997 and seeks to expand its participation in regional economic and trade groups.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="background-color:#b0c4de"><b>Mongolia</b></th></tr><tr style="display:none;"><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/></td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color: #b0c4de;">Mongolian name</th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label" style="font-weight:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Cyrillic_script" class="mw-redirect" title="Mongolian Cyrillic script">Mongolian Cyrillic</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><span title="Mongolian-language text"><span lang="mn">Монгол Улс <br />(Mongol Uls)</span></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label" style="font-weight:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Mongolian_script" title="Mongolian script">Mongolian script</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><span title="Mongolian-language text"><span lang="mn"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1125315459"/><span class="font-mong" style="display:inline-block; font-weight:normal; font-size: 1.25em; line-height: 1.2em; -webkit-writing-mode: vertical-lr; -o-writing-mode: vertical-lr; -ms-writing-mode: tb-lr; writing-mode: tb-lr; writing-mode: vertical-lr; vertical-align:text-top;">ᠮᠣᠩᠭᠣᠯ <br />ᠤᠯᠤᠰ</span></span></span></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox-subbox collapsible collapsed" style="display:inline-table; text-align: left;"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-size: 100%; text-align: left; background-color: #f9ffbc;">Transcriptions</th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label" style="font-weight:normal;"><a href="/wiki/SASM/GNC_romanization#Mongolian" title="SASM/GNC romanization">SASM/GNC</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><span title="Mongolian-language romanization"><span style="font-style: normal" lang="mn-Latn">Mongol Uls</span></span></td></tr></tbody></table></td><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/></tr><tr style="display:none"><td colspan="2">
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The name Mongolia means the "Land of the <a href="/wiki/Mongols" title="Mongols">Mongols</a>" in Latin. The origin of the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_language" title="Mongolian language">Mongolian</a> word <i>"Mongol"</i> (<span lang="mn"><a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%BD%D0%B3%D0%BE%D0%BB#Mongolian" class="extiw" title="wikt:монгол">монгол</a></span>) is of uncertain etymology, given variously such as the name of a mountain or river; a corruption of the Mongolian <i>Mongkhe-<a href="/wiki/Tengri" title="Tengri">tengri</a>-gal</i> ("Eternal Sky Fire");<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> or a derivation from <a href="/wiki/Yujiul%C3%BC_Mugul%C3%BC" class="mw-redirect" title="Yujiulü Mugulü">Mugulü</a>, the 4th-century founder of the <a href="/wiki/Rouran_Khaganate" title="Rouran Khaganate">Rouran Khaganate</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> First attested as the 'Mungu',<sup id="cite_ref-Svanty_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Svanty-18">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> (<a href="/wiki/Chinese_language" title="Chinese language">Chinese</a>: <span lang="zh"><a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E8%92%99%E5%85%80#Chinese" class="extiw" title="wikt:蒙兀">蒙兀</a></span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Pinyin" title="Pinyin">Modern Chinese</a></small> <i>Měngwù</i>, <small><a href="/wiki/Middle_Chinese" title="Middle Chinese">Middle Chinese</a></small> <i>Muwngu</i><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>), a branch of the <a href="/wiki/Shiwei_people" title="Shiwei people">Shiwei</a> in an 8th-century <a href="/wiki/Tang_dynasty" title="Tang dynasty">Tang dynasty</a> list of northern tribes, presumably related to the <a href="/wiki/Liao_dynasty" title="Liao dynasty">Liao</a>-era <i>Mungku</i><sup id="cite_ref-Svanty_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Svanty-18">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> (<a href="/wiki/Chinese_language" title="Chinese language">Chinese</a>: <span lang="zh"><a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E8%92%99%E5%8F%A4#Chinese" class="extiw" title="wikt:蒙古">蒙古</a></span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Pinyin" title="Pinyin">Modern Chinese</a></small> <i>Měnggǔ</i>, <small><a href="/wiki/Middle_Chinese" title="Middle Chinese">Middle Chinese</a></small> <i>MuwngkuX</i><sup id="cite_ref-BaxSag_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BaxSag-20">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>).
</p><p>After the fall of the Liao in 1125, the <a href="/wiki/Khamag_Mongol" title="Khamag Mongol">Khamag Mongols</a> became a leading tribe on the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Plateau" title="Mongolian Plateau">Mongolian Plateau</a>. However, their wars with the <a href="/wiki/Jurchen_people" title="Jurchen people">Jurchen</a>-ruled <a href="/wiki/Jin_dynasty_(1115%E2%80%931234)" title="Jin dynasty (1115–1234)">Jin dynasty</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Tatar_confederation" title="Tatar confederation">Tatar confederation</a> had weakened them. The last head of the tribe was <a href="/wiki/Yes%C3%BCgei" class="mw-redirect" title="Yesügei">Yesügei</a>, whose son <a href="/wiki/Tem%C3%BCjin" class="mw-redirect" title="Temüjin">Temüjin</a> eventually united all the Shiwei tribes as the <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol Empire</a> (<i>Yekhe Monggol Ulus</i>). In the thirteenth century, the word Mongol grew into an umbrella term for a large group of <a href="/wiki/Mongolic_languages" title="Mongolic languages">Mongolic-speaking</a> tribes united under the rule of <a href="/wiki/Genghis_Khan" title="Genghis Khan">Genghis Khan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since the adoption of the new <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Mongolia" title="Constitution of Mongolia">Constitution of Mongolia</a> on 13 February 1992, the official name of the state is "Mongolia" (<i>Mongol Uls</i>).
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Mongolia" title="History of Mongolia">History of Mongolia</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prehistory_and_antiquity">Prehistory and antiquity</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Prehistoric_Mongolia" title="Prehistoric Mongolia">Prehistoric Mongolia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Proto-Mongols" title="Proto-Mongols">Proto-Mongols</a></div>
<p>The <i><a href="/wiki/Khoit_Tsenkher_Cave_Rock_Art" title="Khoit Tsenkher Cave Rock Art">Khoit Tsenkher Cave</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-Novgorodova_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Novgorodova-22">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> in <a href="/wiki/Khovd_Province" title="Khovd Province">Khovd Province</a> shows lively pink, brown, and red ochre paintings (dated to 20,000 years ago) of <a href="/wiki/Mammoths" class="mw-redirect" title="Mammoths">mammoths</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lynx" title="Lynx">lynx</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bactrian_camel" title="Bactrian camel">bactrian camels</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ostrich" title="Ostrich">ostriches</a>, earning it the nickname "the <a href="/wiki/Lascaux" title="Lascaux">Lascaux</a> of Mongolia". The <a href="/wiki/Venus_figurines_of_Mal%27ta" title="Venus figurines of Mal&#39;ta">Venus figurines of Mal'ta</a> (21,000 years ago) testify to the level of Upper Paleolithic art in northern Mongolia; Mal'ta is now part of Russia.
Neolithic agricultural settlements (c. 5500–3500 BC), such as those at Norovlin, Tamsagbulag, Bayanzag, and Rashaan Khad, predated the introduction of horse-riding nomadism, a pivotal event in the history of Mongolia which became the dominant culture. Horse-riding nomadism has been documented by archeological evidence in Mongolia during the Copper and Bronze Age <a href="/wiki/Afanasevo_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="Afanasevo culture">Afanasevo culture</a> (3500–2500 BC);<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> this <a href="/wiki/Indo-European_migrations" title="Indo-European migrations">Indo-European</a> culture was active to the <a href="/wiki/Khangai_Mountains" title="Khangai Mountains">Khangai Mountains</a> in Central Mongolia. The wheeled vehicles found in the burials of the Afanasevans have been dated to before 2200 BC.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Pastoral nomadism and metalworking became more developed with the later <a href="/wiki/Okunev_culture" title="Okunev culture">Okunev culture</a> (2nd millennium BC), <a href="/wiki/Andronovo_culture" title="Andronovo culture">Andronovo culture</a> (2300–1000 BC) and <a href="/wiki/Karasuk_culture" title="Karasuk culture">Karasuk culture</a> (1500–300 BC), culminating with the Iron Age <a href="/wiki/Xiongnu" title="Xiongnu">Xiongnu</a> Empire in 209 BC. Monuments of the pre-Xiongnu Bronze Age include <a href="/wiki/Deer_stone" title="Deer stone">deer stones</a>, keregsur <a href="/wiki/Kurgans" class="mw-redirect" title="Kurgans">kurgans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Slab_Grave_Culture" class="mw-redirect" title="Slab Grave Culture">square slab tombs</a>, and rock paintings.
</p><p>Although cultivation of crops has continued since the Neolithic, agriculture has always remained small in scale compared to pastoral nomadism. Agriculture may have first been introduced from the west or arose independently in the region. The population during the <a href="/wiki/Copper_Age" class="mw-redirect" title="Copper Age">Copper Age</a> has been described as mongoloid in the east of what is now Mongolia, and as europoid in the west.<sup id="cite_ref-Novgorodova_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Novgorodova-22">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Tocharians (<a href="/wiki/Yuezhi" title="Yuezhi">Yuezhi</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Scythians" title="Scythians">Scythians</a> inhabited western Mongolia during the Bronze Age. The mummy of a Scythian warrior, which is believed to be about 2,500 years old, was a 30- to 40-year-old man with blond hair; it was found in <a href="/wiki/Altai_Mountains" title="Altai Mountains">the Altai</a>, Mongolia.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> As equine nomadism was introduced into Mongolia, the political center of the <a href="/wiki/Eurasian_Steppe" title="Eurasian Steppe">Eurasian Steppe</a> also shifted to Mongolia, where it remained until the 18th century CE. The intrusions of northern pastoralists (e.g. the Guifang, Shanrong, and <a href="/wiki/Donghu_people" title="Donghu people">Donghu</a>) into China during the <a href="/wiki/Shang_dynasty" title="Shang dynasty">Shang dynasty</a> (1600–1046 BC) and <a href="/wiki/Zhou_dynasty" title="Zhou dynasty">Zhou dynasty</a> (1046–256 BC) presaged the age of <a href="/wiki/Nomadic_empire" title="Nomadic empire">nomadic empires</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:ZaamarTomb.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/ZaamarTomb.jpg/220px-ZaamarTomb.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/ZaamarTomb.jpg/330px-ZaamarTomb.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/ZaamarTomb.jpg/440px-ZaamarTomb.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1801" data-file-height="1351" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:ZaamarTomb.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>7th-century artifacts found 180&#160;km (112&#160;mi) from Ulaanbaatar.</div></div></div>
<p>Since prehistoric times, Mongolia has been inhabited by nomads who, from time to time, formed great confederations that rose to power and prominence. Common institutions were the office of the <a href="/wiki/Khan_(title)" title="Khan (title)">Khan</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Kurultai" title="Kurultai">Kurultai</a> (Supreme Council), left and right wings, imperial army (<a href="/wiki/Keshig" class="mw-redirect" title="Keshig">Keshig</a>) and the decimal military system. The first of these empires, the <a href="/wiki/Xiongnu" title="Xiongnu">Xiongnu</a> of undetermined ethnicity, were brought together by <a href="/wiki/Modu_Shanyu" class="mw-redirect" title="Modu Shanyu">Modu Shanyu</a> to form a confederation in 209 BC. Soon they emerged as the greatest threat to the <a href="/wiki/Qin_Dynasty" class="mw-redirect" title="Qin Dynasty">Qin Dynasty</a>, forcing the latter to construct the <a href="/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China" title="Great Wall of China">Great Wall of China</a>. It was guarded by up to almost 300,000 soldiers during Marshal <a href="/wiki/Meng_Tian" title="Meng Tian">Meng Tian</a>'s tenure, as a means of defense against the destructive Xiongnu raids. The vast Xiongnu empire (209 BC–93 AD) was followed by the Mongolic <a href="/wiki/Xianbei_state" title="Xianbei state">Xianbei empire</a> (93–234 AD), which also ruled more than the entirety of present-day Mongolia. The Mongolic <a href="/wiki/Rouran" class="mw-redirect" title="Rouran">Rouran</a> Khaganate (330–555), of <a href="/wiki/Xianbei" title="Xianbei">Xianbei</a> provenance was the first to use "Khagan" as an imperial title. It ruled a massive empire before being defeated by the <a href="/wiki/G%C3%B6kt%C3%BCrks" title="Göktürks">Göktürks</a> (555–745) whose empire was even bigger.
</p><p>The Göktürks laid siege to <a href="/wiki/Panticapaeum" title="Panticapaeum">Panticapaeum</a>, present-day <a href="/wiki/Kerch" title="Kerch">Kerch</a>, in 576. They were succeeded by the <a href="/wiki/Uyghur_Khaganate" title="Uyghur Khaganate">Uyghur Khaganate</a> (745–840) who were defeated by the Kyrgyz. The Mongolic <a href="/wiki/Khitan_people" title="Khitan people">Khitans</a>, descendants of the Xianbei, ruled Mongolia during the <a href="/wiki/Liao_Dynasty" class="mw-redirect" title="Liao Dynasty">Liao Dynasty</a> (907–1125), after which the <a href="/wiki/Khamag_Mongol" title="Khamag Mongol">Khamag Mongol</a> (1125–1206) rose to prominence.
</p><p>Lines 3–5 of the memorial inscription of <a href="/wiki/Bilge_Khagan" class="mw-redirect" title="Bilge Khagan">Bilge Khagan</a> (684–737) in central Mongolia summarizes the time of the <a href="/wiki/Khagan" title="Khagan">Khagans</a>:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p> In battles they subdued the nations of all four sides of the world and suppressed them. They made those who had heads bow their heads, and who had knees genuflect them. In the east up to the Kadyrkhan common people, in the west up to the Iron Gate they conquered... These Khagans were wise. These Khagans were great. Their servants were wise and great too. Officials were honest and direct with people. They ruled the nation this way. This way they held sway over them. When they died ambassadors from Bokuli Cholug (Baekje Korea), Tabgach (Tang China), Tibet (Tibetan Empire), <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Avars" title="Pannonian Avars">Avar</a> (Avar Khaganate), Rome (Byzantine Empire), <a href="/wiki/Yenisei_Kirghiz" class="mw-redirect" title="Yenisei Kirghiz">Kirgiz</a>, Uch-Kurykan, Otuz-Tatars, <a href="/wiki/Khitan_people" title="Khitan people">Khitans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kumo_Xi" title="Kumo Xi">Tatabis</a> came to the funerals. So many people came to mourn over the great Khagans. They were famous Khagans.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Middle_Ages_to_early_20th_century">Middle Ages to early 20th century</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol Empire</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mongolia_under_Yuan_rule" title="Mongolia under Yuan rule">Mongolia under Yuan rule</a>, <a href="/wiki/Northern_Yuan_dynasty" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Yuan dynasty">Northern Yuan dynasty</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dzungar_Khanate" title="Dzungar Khanate">Dzungar Khanate</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Mongolia_under_Qing_rule" title="Mongolia under Qing rule">Mongolia under Qing rule</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_medieval_Mongol_tribes_and_clans" title="List of medieval Mongol tribes and clans">List of medieval Mongol tribes and clans</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_Mongol_states" title="List of Mongol states">List of Mongol states</a>, and <a href="/wiki/List_of_Mongol_rulers" title="List of Mongol rulers">List of Mongol rulers</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongol_Empire_map.gif" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Mongol_Empire_map.gif/220px-Mongol_Empire_map.gif" decoding="async" width="220" height="173" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Mongol_Empire_map.gif/330px-Mongol_Empire_map.gif 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Mongol_Empire_map.gif/440px-Mongol_Empire_map.gif 2x" data-file-width="752" data-file-height="591" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongol_Empire_map.gif" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol Empire</a> expansion (1206 till 1294)</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:1236-1242_Mongol_invasions_of_Europe.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/1236-1242_Mongol_invasions_of_Europe.jpg/220px-1236-1242_Mongol_invasions_of_Europe.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="129" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/1236-1242_Mongol_invasions_of_Europe.jpg/330px-1236-1242_Mongol_invasions_of_Europe.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/1236-1242_Mongol_invasions_of_Europe.jpg/440px-1236-1242_Mongol_invasions_of_Europe.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2510" data-file-height="1472" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:1236-1242_Mongol_invasions_of_Europe.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>1236-1242 Mongol invasions of Europe</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongols-map.png" class="image"><img alt="Map of Asia" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Mongols-map.png/220px-Mongols-map.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="159" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Mongols-map.png/330px-Mongols-map.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Mongols-map.png/440px-Mongols-map.png 2x" data-file-width="728" data-file-height="525" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongols-map.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>This map shows the boundary of the 13th-century <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol Empire</a> compared to today's <a href="/wiki/Mongols" title="Mongols">Mongols</a>. The red area shows where the majority of <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_language" title="Mongolian language">Mongolian</a> speakers reside today.</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Northern_Yuan.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/Northern_Yuan.png/220px-Northern_Yuan.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="184" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/Northern_Yuan.png/330px-Northern_Yuan.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c3/Northern_Yuan.png 2x" data-file-width="405" data-file-height="339" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Northern_Yuan.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Northern Yuan at its greatest extent.</div></div></div>
<p>In the chaos of the late 12th century, a chieftain named <a href="/wiki/Genghis_Khan" title="Genghis Khan">Temüjin</a> finally succeeded in uniting the Mongol tribes between <a href="/wiki/Manchuria" title="Manchuria">Manchuria</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Altai_Mountains" title="Altai Mountains">Altai Mountains</a>. In 1206, he took the title <a href="/wiki/Genghis_Khan#Name_and_title" title="Genghis Khan">Genghis Khan</a>, and waged a series of military campaigns – renowned for their brutality and ferocity – sweeping through much of Asia, and forming the <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol Empire</a>, the largest contiguous land empire in world history. Under his successors it stretched from present-day <a href="/wiki/Poland" title="Poland">Poland</a> in the west to <a href="/wiki/Korea" title="Korea">Korea</a> in the east, and from parts of <a href="/wiki/Siberia" title="Siberia">Siberia</a> in the north to the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Oman" title="Gulf of Oman">Gulf of Oman</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vietnam" title="Vietnam">Vietnam</a> in the south, covering some 33,000,000 square kilometres (13,000,000&#160;sq&#160;mi),<sup id="cite_ref-EarthRule_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EarthRule-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> (22% of Earth's total land area) and had a population of over 100 million people (about a quarter of Earth's total population at the time). The emergence of <a href="/wiki/Pax_Mongolica" title="Pax Mongolica">Pax Mongolica</a> also significantly eased trade and commerce across Asia during its height.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After Genghis Khan's death, the empire was subdivided into four kingdoms or <a href="/wiki/Khanate" title="Khanate">Khanates</a>. These eventually became quasi-independent after the <a href="/wiki/Toluid_Civil_War" title="Toluid Civil War">Toluid Civil War</a> (1260–1264), which broke out in a battle for power following <a href="/wiki/M%C3%B6ngke_Khan" title="Möngke Khan">Möngke Khan</a>'s death in 1259. One of the khanates, the "Great Khaanate", consisting of the Mongol homeland and most of modern-day <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a>, became known as the <a href="/wiki/Yuan_dynasty" title="Yuan dynasty">Yuan dynasty</a> under <a href="/wiki/Kublai_Khan" title="Kublai Khan">Kublai Khan</a>, the grandson of Genghis Khan. He set up his capital in present-day <a href="/wiki/Beijing" title="Beijing">Beijing</a>. After more than a century of power, the Yuan dynasty was overthrown by the <a href="/wiki/Ming_dynasty" title="Ming dynasty">Ming dynasty</a> in 1368, and the Yuan court fled to the north, thus becoming the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Yuan" title="Northern Yuan">Northern Yuan dynasty</a>. As the Ming armies pursued the Mongols into their homeland, they successfully sacked and destroyed the Mongol capital <a href="/wiki/Karakorum" title="Karakorum">Karakorum</a> and other cities. Some of these attacks were repelled by the Mongols under <a href="/wiki/Biligt%C3%BC_Khan_Ayushiridara" title="Biligtü Khan Ayushiridara">Ayushridar</a> and his general <a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ke_Tem%C3%BCr" title="Köke Temür">Köke Temür</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the expulsion of the Yuan rulers from <a href="/wiki/China_proper" title="China proper">China proper</a>, the Mongols continued to rule their homeland, known in historiography as the Northern Yuan dynasty. The next centuries were marked by violent power struggles among various factions, notably the Genghisids and the non-Genghisid <a href="/wiki/Oirats" title="Oirats">Oirats</a>, as well as by several Ming invasions (such as the <a href="/wiki/Yongle_Emperor%27s_campaigns_against_the_Mongols" title="Yongle Emperor&#39;s campaigns against the Mongols">five expeditions led by the Yongle Emperor</a>).
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:182px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Genghis_khan.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Genghis_khan.jpg/180px-Genghis_khan.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="241" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Genghis_khan.jpg/270px-Genghis_khan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Genghis_khan.jpg/360px-Genghis_khan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="505" data-file-height="676" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Genghis_khan.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Genghis Khan, the first Mongol Emperor</div></div></div>
<p>In the early 16th century, <a href="/wiki/Dayan_Khan" title="Dayan Khan">Dayan Khan</a> and his <a href="/wiki/Khatun" title="Khatun">khatun</a> <a href="/wiki/Mandukhai" title="Mandukhai">Mandukhai</a> reunited the entire Mongol nation under the Genghisids. In the mid-16th century, <a href="/wiki/Altan_Khan" title="Altan Khan">Altan Khan</a> of the Tümed, a grandson of Dayan Khan – but not a hereditary or legitimate Khan – became powerful. He founded <a href="/wiki/Hohhot" title="Hohhot">Hohhot</a> in 1557. After he met with the <a href="/wiki/Dalai_Lama" title="Dalai Lama">Dalai Lama</a> in 1578, he ordered the introduction of <a href="/wiki/Tibetan_Buddhism" title="Tibetan Buddhism">Tibetan Buddhism</a> to Mongolia. (It was the second time this had occurred.) Abtai Khan of the <a href="/wiki/Khalkha" class="mw-redirect" title="Khalkha">Khalkha</a> converted to Buddhism and founded the <a href="/wiki/Erdene_Zuu" class="mw-redirect" title="Erdene Zuu">Erdene Zuu</a> monastery in 1585. His grandson <a href="/wiki/Zanabazar" title="Zanabazar">Zanabazar</a> became the first <a href="/wiki/Jebtsundamba_Khutughtu" class="mw-redirect" title="Jebtsundamba Khutughtu">Jebtsundamba Khutughtu</a> in 1640. Following the leaders, the entire Mongolian population embraced Buddhism. Each family kept scriptures and Buddha statues on an altar at the north side of their <a href="/wiki/Yurt" title="Yurt">ger</a> (yurt). Mongolian nobles donated land, money and herders to the monasteries. As was typical in states with established religions, the top religious institutions, the monasteries, wielded significant temporal power in addition to spiritual power.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2013)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The last Mongol Khan was <a href="/wiki/Ligden_Khan" class="mw-redirect" title="Ligden Khan">Ligden Khan</a> in the early 17th century. He came into conflicts with the <a href="/wiki/Manchu_people" title="Manchu people">Manchus</a> over the looting of Chinese cities, and also alienated most Mongol tribes. He died in 1634. By 1636 most <a href="/wiki/Inner_Mongolia" title="Inner Mongolia">Inner Mongolian</a> tribes had submitted to the Manchus, who founded the <a href="/wiki/Qing_dynasty" title="Qing dynasty">Qing dynasty</a>. The Khalkha eventually submitted to Qing rule in 1691, thus bringing all of today's Mongolia under Manchu rule. After several <a href="/wiki/Dzungar%E2%80%93Qing_Wars" title="Dzungar–Qing Wars">Dzungar–Qing Wars</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Dzungars" class="mw-redirect" title="Dzungars">Dzungars</a> (western Mongols or Oirats) were virtually annihilated during the Qing conquest of Dzungaria in 1757 and 1758.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:152px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Altan_Khan.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/76/Altan_Khan.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="232" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="150" data-file-height="232" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Altan_Khan.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Altan_Khan" title="Altan Khan">Altan Khan</a> (1507–1582) founded the city of <a href="/wiki/Hohhot" title="Hohhot">Hohhot</a>, helped introduce Buddhism and originated the title of <a href="/wiki/Dalai_Lama" title="Dalai Lama">Dalai Lama</a></div></div></div>
<p>Some scholars estimate that about 80% of the 600,000 or more <a href="/wiki/Dzungar_genocide" title="Dzungar genocide">Dzungar were destroyed</a> by a combination of disease and warfare.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Outer Mongolia was given relative autonomy, being administered by the hereditary Genghisid khanates of Tusheet Khan, Setsen Khan, Zasagt Khan and Sain Noyon Khan. The <a href="/wiki/Jebtsundamba_Khutuktu" title="Jebtsundamba Khutuktu">Jebtsundamba Khutuktu</a> of Mongolia had immense <i>de facto</i> authority. The Manchu forbade mass Chinese immigration into the area, which allowed the Mongols to keep their culture. The Oirats who migrated to the <a href="/wiki/Pontic%E2%80%93Caspian_steppe" title="Pontic–Caspian steppe">Volga steppes</a> in Russia became known as <a href="/wiki/Kalmyks" title="Kalmyks">Kalmyks</a>.
</p><p>The main trade route during this period was the <a href="/wiki/Siberian_Route" title="Siberian Route">Tea Road</a> through Siberia; it had permanent stations located every 25 to 30 kilometres (16 to 19&#160;mi), each of which was staffed by 5–30 chosen families.
</p><p>Until 1911, the Qing dynasty maintained control of Mongolia with a series of alliances and intermarriages, as well as military and economic measures. <a href="/wiki/Amban" title="Amban">Ambans</a>, Manchu "high officials", were installed in <a href="/wiki/Ulaanbaatar" title="Ulaanbaatar">Khüree</a>, <a href="/wiki/Uliastai" title="Uliastai">Uliastai</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Khovd_(city)" title="Khovd (city)">Khovd</a>, and the country was divided into numerous feudal and ecclesiastical fiefdoms (which also placed people in power with loyalty to the Qing). Over the course of the 19th century, the feudal lords attached more importance to representation and less importance to the responsibilities towards their subjects. The behaviour of Mongolia's nobility, together with <a href="/wiki/Usury" title="Usury">usurious</a> practices by Chinese traders and the collection of imperial taxes in silver instead of animals, resulted in widespread poverty among the nomads. By 1911 there were 700 large and small monasteries in Outer Mongolia; their 115,000 monks made up 21% of the population. Apart from the Jebtsundamba Khutuktu, there were 13 other reincarnating high lamas, called 'seal-holding saints' (<i>tamgatai khutuktu</i>), in Outer Mongolia.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Modern_history">Modern history</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Revolution_of_1911" title="Mongolian Revolution of 1911">Mongolian Revolution of 1911</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bogd_Khanate_of_Mongolia" title="Bogd Khanate of Mongolia">Bogd Khanate of Mongolia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Revolution_of_1921" title="Mongolian Revolution of 1921">Mongolian Revolution of 1921</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Republic" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Republic">Mongolian People's Republic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Revolution_of_1990" title="Mongolian Revolution of 1990">Mongolian Revolution of 1990</a>, and <a href="/wiki/History_of_modern_Mongolia" title="History of modern Mongolia">History of modern Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BogdKhan.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/BogdKhan.jpg/170px-BogdKhan.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="282" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/BogdKhan.jpg/255px-BogdKhan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/60/BogdKhan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="498" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BogdKhan.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The eighth <a href="/wiki/Jebtsundamba_Khutuktu" title="Jebtsundamba Khutuktu">Jebtsundamba Khutuktu</a>, Bogd Khaan</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Map-of-Unified-Mongolia-1917.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/78/Map-of-Unified-Mongolia-1917.jpg/220px-Map-of-Unified-Mongolia-1917.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/78/Map-of-Unified-Mongolia-1917.jpg/330px-Map-of-Unified-Mongolia-1917.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/78/Map-of-Unified-Mongolia-1917.jpg/440px-Map-of-Unified-Mongolia-1917.jpg 2x" data-file-width="9147" data-file-height="6199" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Map-of-Unified-Mongolia-1917.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of unified Mongolia in 1917</div></div></div>
<p>With the fall of the Qing dynasty in 1911, Mongolia under the <a href="/wiki/Bogd_Khaan" class="mw-redirect" title="Bogd Khaan">Bogd Khaan</a> declared its independence. But the newly established <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_China_(1912%E2%80%931949)" title="Republic of China (1912–1949)">Republic of China</a> considered Mongolia to be part of its own territory. <a href="/wiki/Yuan_Shikai" title="Yuan Shikai">Yuan Shikai</a>, the <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_Republic_of_China" title="President of the Republic of China">President of the Republic of China</a>, considered the new republic to be the <a href="/wiki/Successor_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Successor state">successor</a> of the Qing. Bogd Khaan said that <a href="/wiki/Qing_dynasty_in_Inner_Asia" title="Qing dynasty in Inner Asia">both Mongolia and China</a> had been administered by the <a href="/wiki/Manchu_people" title="Manchu people">Manchu</a> during the Qing, and after the fall of the Qing dynasty in 1911, the contract of Mongolian submission to the Manchu had become invalid.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;d&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The area controlled by the Bogd Khaan was approximately that of the former <a href="/wiki/Outer_Mongolia" title="Outer Mongolia">Outer Mongolia</a> during the Qing period. In 1919, after the <a href="/wiki/October_Revolution" title="October Revolution">October Revolution</a> in Russia, Chinese troops led by <a href="/wiki/Warlord_Era" title="Warlord Era">warlord</a> <a href="/wiki/Xu_Shuzheng" title="Xu Shuzheng">Xu Shuzheng</a> occupied Mongolia. Warfare erupted on the northern border. As a result of the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Civil_War" title="Russian Civil War">Russian Civil War</a>, the <a href="/wiki/White_movement" title="White movement">White</a> Russian <a href="/wiki/Lieutenant_General" class="mw-redirect" title="Lieutenant General">Lieutenant General</a> <a href="/wiki/Roman_Ungern_von_Sternberg" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Ungern von Sternberg">Baron Ungern</a> led his troops into Mongolia in October 1920, defeating the Chinese forces in <a href="/wiki/Ulaanbaatar" title="Ulaanbaatar">Niislel Khüree</a> (now Ulaanbaatar) in early February 1921 with Mongol support.
</p><p>To eliminate the threat posed by Ungern, <a href="/wiki/Russian_SFSR" class="mw-redirect" title="Russian SFSR">Bolshevik Russia</a> decided to support the establishment of a communist Mongolian government and army. This Mongolian army took the <a href="/wiki/Altanbulag,_Selenge" title="Altanbulag, Selenge">Mongolian part</a> of <a href="/wiki/Kyakhta" title="Kyakhta">Kyakhta</a> from Chinese forces on 18 March 1921, and on 6 July, Russian and Mongolian troops arrived in Khüree. Mongolia declared its independence again on 11 July 1921.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> As a result, Mongolia was closely aligned with the Soviet Union over the next seven decades.
</p>
<h4><span id="Mongolian_People.27s_Republic"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Mongolian_People's_Republic">Mongolian People's Republic</span></h4>
<p>In 1924, after the <a href="/wiki/Bogd_Khaan" class="mw-redirect" title="Bogd Khaan">Bogd Khaan</a> died of <a href="/wiki/Laryngeal_cancer" title="Laryngeal cancer">laryngeal cancer</a><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> or, as some sources claim, at the hands of Russian spies,<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> the country's <a href="/wiki/Political_system" title="Political system">political system</a> was changed. The <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Republic" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Republic">Mongolian People's Republic</a> was established. In 1928, <a href="/wiki/Khorloogiin_Choibalsan" title="Khorloogiin Choibalsan">Khorloogiin Choibalsan</a> rose to power. The early leaders of the Mongolian People's Republic (1921–1952) included many with <a href="/wiki/Pan-Mongolism" title="Pan-Mongolism">Pan-Mongolist</a> ideals. However, changing global politics and increased Soviet pressure led to the decline of Pan-Mongol aspirations in the following period.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:152px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Horloogiyn_Choybalsan.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Horloogiyn_Choybalsan.jpg/150px-Horloogiyn_Choybalsan.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="205" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Horloogiyn_Choybalsan.jpg/225px-Horloogiyn_Choybalsan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Horloogiyn_Choybalsan.jpg/300px-Horloogiyn_Choybalsan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="439" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Horloogiyn_Choybalsan.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Khorloogiin_Choibalsan" title="Khorloogiin Choibalsan">Khorloogiin Choibalsan</a> led Mongolia during the <a href="/wiki/Stalinist_era" class="mw-redirect" title="Stalinist era">Stalinist era</a> and presided over an environment of intense political persecution</div></div></div>
<p>Khorloogiin Choibalsan instituted <a href="/wiki/Collectivization" class="mw-redirect" title="Collectivization">collectivization</a> of livestock, began the destruction of the <a href="/wiki/Tibetan_Buddhism" title="Tibetan Buddhism">Buddhist</a> <a href="/wiki/Monastery" title="Monastery">monasteries</a>, and carried out <a href="/wiki/Stalinist_repressions_in_Mongolia" title="Stalinist repressions in Mongolia">Stalinist purges</a>, which resulted in the murders of numerous monks and other leaders. In Mongolia during the 1920s, approximately one-third of the male population were monks. By the beginning of the 20th century, about 750 monasteries were functioning in Mongolia.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1930, the Soviet Union stopped <a href="/wiki/Buryats" title="Buryats">Buryat</a> migration to the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Republic" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Republic">Mongolian People's Republic</a> to prevent Mongolian reunification. All leaders of Mongolia who did not fulfill <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Stalin" title="Joseph Stalin">Stalin</a>'s demands to perform <a href="/wiki/Red_Terror" title="Red Terror">Red Terror</a> against Mongolians were executed, including <a href="/wiki/Peljidiin_Genden" title="Peljidiin Genden">Peljidiin Genden</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anandyn_Amar" title="Anandyn Amar">Anandyn Amar</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Great_Purge" title="Great Purge">Stalinist purges in Mongolia</a>, which began in 1937, killed more than 30,000 people. Choibalsan died suspiciously in the Soviet Union in 1952. <a href="/wiki/Comintern" class="mw-redirect" title="Comintern">Comintern</a> leader <a href="/wiki/Bohum%C3%ADr_%C5%A0meral" title="Bohumír Šmeral">Bohumír Šmeral</a> said, "People of Mongolia are not important, the land is important. Mongolian land is larger than England, France and Germany".<sup id="cite_ref-Mongolia_2003_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mongolia_2003-39">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific page or range of pages in which the material appears. (August 2021)">page&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:MNRA_soldiers_1939.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/MNRA_soldiers_1939.jpg/220px-MNRA_soldiers_1939.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/MNRA_soldiers_1939.jpg/330px-MNRA_soldiers_1939.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/MNRA_soldiers_1939.jpg/440px-MNRA_soldiers_1939.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:MNRA_soldiers_1939.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mongolian troops fight against the Japanese counterattack at <a href="/wiki/Battles_of_Khalkhin_Gol" title="Battles of Khalkhin Gol">Khalkhin Gol</a>, 1939</div></div></div>
<p>After the <a href="/wiki/Japanese_invasion_of_Manchuria" title="Japanese invasion of Manchuria">Japanese invasion of neighboring Manchuria</a> in 1931, Mongolia was threatened on this front. During the <a href="/wiki/Battles_of_Khalkhin_Gol" title="Battles of Khalkhin Gol">Soviet-Japanese Border War</a> of 1939, the Soviet Union successfully defended Mongolia against Japanese expansionism. Mongolia fought against Japan during the <a href="/wiki/Battles_of_Khalkhin_Gol" title="Battles of Khalkhin Gol">Battles of Khalkhin Gol</a> in 1939 and during the <a href="/wiki/Soviet%E2%80%93Japanese_War" title="Soviet–Japanese War">Soviet–Japanese War</a> in August 1945 to liberate <a href="/wiki/Inner_Mongolia" title="Inner Mongolia">Inner Mongolia</a> from Japan and <a href="/wiki/Mengjiang" title="Mengjiang">Mengjiang</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Cold_War">Cold War</span></h4>
<p>The February 1945 <a href="/wiki/Yalta_Conference" title="Yalta Conference">Yalta Conference</a> provided for the Soviet Union's participation in the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_War" title="Pacific War">Pacific War</a>. One of the Soviet conditions for its participation, put forward at Yalta, was that after the war Outer Mongolia would retain its independence. <a href="/wiki/1945_Mongolian_independence_referendum" title="1945 Mongolian independence referendum">The referendum</a> took place on 20 October 1945, with (according to official numbers) 100% of the electorate voting for independence.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the establishment of the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Republic_of_China" class="mw-redirect" title="People&#39;s Republic of China">People's Republic of China</a>, both countries confirmed their mutual recognition on 6 October 1949. However, the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_China" class="mw-redirect" title="Republic of China">Republic of China</a> used its <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_veto_power" title="United Nations Security Council veto power">Security Council veto</a> in 1955, to stop the admission of the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Republic" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Republic">Mongolian People's Republic</a> to the United Nations on the grounds it recognized all of Mongolia —including Outer Mongolia— as part of China. This was the only time the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_China_and_the_United_Nations" class="mw-redirect" title="Republic of China and the United Nations">Republic of China</a> ever used its veto. Hence, and because of the repeated threats to veto by the ROC, Mongolia did not join the UN until 1961 when the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a> agreed to lift its veto on the admission of <a href="/wiki/Mauritania" title="Mauritania">Mauritania</a> (and any other newly independent African state), in return for the admission of Mongolia. Faced with pressure from nearly all the other African countries, the ROC relented under protest. Mongolia and Mauritania were both admitted to the UN on 27 October 1961.<sup id="cite_ref-unveto_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unveto-42">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-bbcveto_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbcveto-43">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-gpveto_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gpveto-44">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> (see <a href="/wiki/China_and_the_United_Nations" title="China and the United Nations">China and the United Nations</a>)
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:152px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Yumjaagiin_Tsedenbal_Berlin,_VIII.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Yumjaagiin_Tsedenbal_Berlin%2C_VIII.jpg/150px-Yumjaagiin_Tsedenbal_Berlin%2C_VIII.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="148" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Yumjaagiin_Tsedenbal_Berlin%2C_VIII.jpg/225px-Yumjaagiin_Tsedenbal_Berlin%2C_VIII.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/e2/Yumjaagiin_Tsedenbal_Berlin%2C_VIII.jpg 2x" data-file-width="282" data-file-height="279" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Yumjaagiin_Tsedenbal_Berlin,_VIII.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mongolian Premier <a href="/wiki/Yumjaagiin_Tsedenbal" title="Yumjaagiin Tsedenbal">Yumjaagiin Tsedenbal</a> was the longest-serving leader in the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Bloc" class="mw-redirect" title="Soviet Bloc">Soviet Bloc</a>, with over 44 years in office</div></div></div>
<p>On 26 January 1952, <a href="/wiki/Yumjaagiin_Tsedenbal" title="Yumjaagiin Tsedenbal">Yumjaagiin Tsedenbal</a> took power in Mongolia after the death of Choibalsan. Tsedenbal was the leading political figure in Mongolia for more than 30 years.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> While Tsedenbal was visiting Moscow in August 1984, his severe illness prompted the parliament to announce his retirement and replace him with <a href="/wiki/Jambyn_Batm%C3%B6nkh" title="Jambyn Batmönkh">Jambyn Batmönkh</a>.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-Cold_War">Post-Cold War</span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Fall_of_the_Soviet_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Fall of the Soviet Union">fall of the Soviet Union</a> in 1991 strongly influenced Mongolian politics and <a href="/wiki/Youth_in_Mongolia" title="Youth in Mongolia">youth</a>. Its people undertook the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Revolution_of_1990" title="Mongolian Revolution of 1990">peaceful Democratic Revolution</a> in <a href="/wiki/Revolutions_of_1989" title="Revolutions of 1989">January 1990</a> and the introduction of a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Market_economy" title="Market economy">market economy</a>. At the same time, the transformation of the former <a href="/wiki/Marxist-Leninist" class="mw-redirect" title="Marxist-Leninist">Marxist-Leninist</a> <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Party" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Party">Mongolian People's Revolutionary Party</a> to the current social democratic <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Party" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Party">Mongolian People's Party</a> reshaped the country's political landscape.
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Mongolia" title="Constitution of Mongolia">new constitution</a> was introduced in 1992, and the term "People's Republic" was dropped from the country's name. The <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Mongolia#Transition_to_a_market_economy" title="Economy of Mongolia">transition to a market economy</a> was often rocky; during the early 1990s the country had to deal with high inflation and food shortages.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> The first election victories for non-communist parties came in 1993 (presidential elections) and 1996 (parliamentary elections). China has supported Mongolia's application for membership in to the <a href="/wiki/Asia_Cooperation_Dialogue" title="Asia Cooperation Dialogue">Asia Cooperation Dialogue</a> (ACD), <a href="/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation" title="Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation">Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation</a> (APEC) and granting it <a href="/wiki/Observer_status" title="Observer status">observer status</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Shanghai_Cooperation_Organisation" title="Shanghai Cooperation Organisation">Shanghai Cooperation Organisation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography_and_climate">Geography and climate</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Mongolia" title="Geography of Mongolia">Geography of Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolia_1996_CIA_map.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Mongolia_1996_CIA_map.jpg/220px-Mongolia_1996_CIA_map.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="181" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Mongolia_1996_CIA_map.jpg/330px-Mongolia_1996_CIA_map.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Mongolia_1996_CIA_map.jpg/440px-Mongolia_1996_CIA_map.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="1649" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolia_1996_CIA_map.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The southern portion of Mongolia is taken up by the <a href="/wiki/Gobi_Desert" title="Gobi Desert">Gobi Desert</a>, while the northern and western portions are mountainous.</div></div></div>
<p>At 1,564,116&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (603,909&#160;sq&#160;mi), Mongolia is the world's <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependencies_by_area" class="mw-redirect" title="List of sovereign states and dependencies by area">18th-largest</a> country (after <a href="/wiki/Iran" title="Iran">Iran</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> It is significantly larger than the next-largest country, <a href="/wiki/Peru" title="Peru">Peru</a>. It mostly lies between latitudes <a href="/wiki/41st_parallel_north" title="41st parallel north">41°</a> and <a href="/wiki/52nd_parallel_north" title="52nd parallel north">52°N</a> (a small area is north of 52°), and longitudes <a href="/wiki/87th_meridian_east" title="87th meridian east">87°</a> and <a href="/wiki/120th_meridian_east" title="120th meridian east">120°E</a>. As a point of reference the northernmost part of Mongolia is on roughly the same latitude as Berlin (Germany) and Saskatoon (Canada), while the southernmost part is on roughly the same latitude as Rome (Italy) and Chicago (USA). The westernmost part of Mongolia is on roughly the same longitude as Kolkata in India, while the easternmost part is on the same longitude as Qinhuangdao and Hangzhou in China, as well as the western edge of Taiwan. Although Mongolia does not share a border with <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan" title="Kazakhstan">Kazakhstan</a>, its westernmost point is only 36.76 kilometres (22.84&#160;mi) from <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan" title="Kazakhstan">Kazakhstan</a>.
</p><p>The geography of Mongolia is varied, with the <a href="/wiki/Gobi_Desert" title="Gobi Desert">Gobi Desert</a> to the south and cold, mountainous regions to the north and west. Much of Mongolia consists of the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian-Manchurian_grassland" class="mw-redirect" title="Mongolian-Manchurian grassland">Mongolian-Manchurian grassland</a> <a href="/wiki/Steppe" title="Steppe">steppe</a>, with forested areas accounting for 11.2% of the total land area,<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> a higher percentage than Ireland (10%).<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The whole of Mongolia is considered to be part of the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Plateau" title="Mongolian Plateau">Mongolian Plateau</a>. The highest point in Mongolia is the <a href="/wiki/Kh%C3%BCiten_Peak" title="Khüiten Peak">Khüiten Peak</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Tavan_bogd" class="mw-redirect" title="Tavan bogd">Tavan bogd</a> massif in the far west at 4,374&#160;m (14,350&#160;ft). The basin of the <a href="/wiki/Uvs_Lake" title="Uvs Lake">Uvs Lake</a>, shared with <a href="/wiki/Tuva_Republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Tuva Republic">Tuva Republic</a> in Russia, is a natural <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Mongolia" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate of Mongolia">Climate of Mongolia</a></div>
<p>Mongolia is known as the "Land of the Eternal Blue Sky" or "Country of Blue Sky" (<a href="/wiki/Mongolian_language" title="Mongolian language">Mongolian</a>: "Mönkh khökh tengeriin oron") because it has over 250 sunny days a year.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_MNG_present.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Koppen-Geiger_Map_MNG_present.svg/220px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_MNG_present.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="106" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Koppen-Geiger_Map_MNG_present.svg/330px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_MNG_present.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Koppen-Geiger_Map_MNG_present.svg/440px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_MNG_present.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1769" data-file-height="850" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_MNG_present.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mongolia map of Köppen climate classification zones.</div></div></div>
<p>Most of the country is hot in the summer and extremely cold in the winter, with January averages dropping as low as −30&#160;°C (−22&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-mong26_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mong26-55">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> A vast front of cold, heavy, shallow air comes in from Siberia in winter and collects in river valleys and low basins causing very cold temperatures while slopes of mountains are much warmer due to the effects of <a href="/wiki/Temperature_inversion" class="mw-redirect" title="Temperature inversion">temperature inversion</a> (temperature increases with altitude).
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Gorkhi_Terelj_Park.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Gorkhi_Terelj_Park.jpg/220px-Gorkhi_Terelj_Park.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Gorkhi_Terelj_Park.jpg/330px-Gorkhi_Terelj_Park.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Gorkhi_Terelj_Park.jpg/440px-Gorkhi_Terelj_Park.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Gorkhi_Terelj_Park.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Khentii_Mountains" title="Khentii Mountains">Khentii Mountains</a> in <a href="/wiki/Gorkhi-Terelj_National_Park" title="Gorkhi-Terelj National Park">Terelj</a>, close to the birthplace of Genghis Khan.</div></div></div>
<p>In winter the whole of Mongolia comes under the influence of the <a href="/wiki/Siberian_Anticyclone" class="mw-redirect" title="Siberian Anticyclone">Siberian Anticyclone</a>. The localities most severely affected by this cold weather are Uvs province (<a href="/wiki/Ulaangom" title="Ulaangom">Ulaangom</a>), western Khovsgol (<a href="/wiki/Rinchinlkhumbe,_Khovsgol" class="mw-redirect" title="Rinchinlkhumbe, Khovsgol">Rinchinlhumbe</a>), eastern Zavkhan (<a href="/wiki/Tosontsengel,_Zavkhan" title="Tosontsengel, Zavkhan">Tosontsengel</a>), northern Bulgan (Hutag) and eastern Dornod province (Khalkhiin Gol). Ulaanbaatar is strongly, but less severely, affected. The cold gets less severe as one goes south, reaching the warmest January temperatures in Omnogovi Province (<a href="/wiki/Dalanzadgad" title="Dalanzadgad">Dalanzadgad</a>, <a href="/wiki/Khanbogd,_%C3%96mn%C3%B6govi" title="Khanbogd, Ömnögovi">Khanbogd</a>) and the region of the Altai mountains bordering China. A unique microclimate is the fertile grassland-forest region of central and eastern Arkhangai Province (<a href="/wiki/Tsetserleg_(city)" title="Tsetserleg (city)">Tsetserleg</a>) and northern Ovorkhangai Province (<a href="/wiki/Arvaikheer" title="Arvaikheer">Arvaikheer</a>) where January temperatures are on average the same and often higher than the warmest desert regions to the south in addition to being more stable. The <a href="/wiki/Khangai_Mountains" title="Khangai Mountains">Khangai Mountains</a> play a certain role in forming this microclimate. In Tsetserleg, the warmest town in this microclimate, nighttime January temperatures rarely go under −30&#160;°C (−22&#160;°F) while daytime January temperatures often reach 0&#160;°C (32&#160;°F) to 5&#160;°C (41&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country is subject to occasional harsh climatic conditions known as <i><a href="/wiki/Zud" title="Zud">zud</a>.</i> Zud, a natural disaster unique to Mongolia, results in large proportions of the country's livestock dying from starvation or freezing temperatures or both, resulting in economic upheaval for the largely pastoral population. The annual average temperature in Ulaanbaatar is −1.3&#160;°C (29.7&#160;°F), making it the world's coldest capital city.<sup id="cite_ref-mong26_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mong26-55">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Mongolia is high, cold and windy.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-58">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> It has an extreme continental climate with long, cold winters and short summers, during which most of its annual precipitation falls.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_58-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-58">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The country averages 257 cloudless days a year, and it is usually at the center of a region of high atmospheric pressure.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_58-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-58">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Precipitation is highest in the north (average of 200 to 350 millimeters (8 to 14&#160;in) per year) and lowest in the south, which receives 100 to 200 millimeters (4 to 8&#160;in) annually.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_58-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-58">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The highest annual precipitation of 622.297&#160;mm (24.500&#160;in) occurred in the forests of <a href="/wiki/Bulgan_Province" title="Bulgan Province">Bulgan Province</a> near the border with Russia and the lowest of 41.735&#160;mm (1.643&#160;in) occurred in the Gobi Desert (period 1961–1990).<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The sparsely populated far north of Bulgan Province averages 600&#160;mm (24&#160;in) in annual precipitation which means it receives more precipitation than Beijing (571.8&#160;mm or 22.51&#160;in) or <a href="/wiki/Berlin" title="Berlin">Berlin</a> (571&#160;mm or 22.5&#160;in).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environmental_issues">Environmental issues</span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066933788">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_Mongolia" title="Environmental issues in Mongolia">Environmental issues in Mongolia</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Environmental_issues_in_Mongolia&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
There are many pressing environmental issues in Mongolia that are detrimental to both human and environmental wellness. These problems have arisen in part due to natural factors, but increasingly because of human actions. One of these issues is <a href="/wiki/Climate_change" title="Climate change">climate change</a>, which will be responsible for an increase in <a href="/wiki/Desertification" title="Desertification">desertification</a>, natural disasters, and <a href="/wiki/Land_degradation" title="Land degradation">land degradation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Another is <a href="/wiki/Deforestation" title="Deforestation">deforestation</a>, which is expanding due to human recklessness, pests, disease, and fires. Mongolian lands are becoming more arid through desertification, a process that is being exacerbated due to irresponsible <a href="/wiki/Land_use" title="Land use">land use</a>. Additionally, more and more species are disappearing and at risk for extinction. Moreover, especially in population centers, Mongolians deal with <a href="/wiki/Air_pollution" title="Air pollution">air</a> and <a href="/wiki/Water_pollution" title="Water pollution">water pollution</a> caused by <a href="/wiki/Industrialization" class="mw-redirect" title="Industrialization">industrialization</a>.</div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Wildlife">Wildlife</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_of_Mongolia" title="Wildlife of Mongolia">Wildlife of Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:KhongorynElsCamels.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/KhongorynElsCamels.jpg/220px-KhongorynElsCamels.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="122" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/KhongorynElsCamels.jpg/330px-KhongorynElsCamels.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/KhongorynElsCamels.jpg/440px-KhongorynElsCamels.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1755" data-file-height="975" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:KhongorynElsCamels.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Bactrian_camels" class="mw-redirect" title="Bactrian camels">Bactrian camels</a> by sand dunes in Gobi Desert.</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Jurty_na_stepie_pomi%C4%99dzy_U%C5%82an_Bator_a_Karakorum_03.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Jurty_na_stepie_pomi%C4%99dzy_U%C5%82an_Bator_a_Karakorum_03.JPG/220px-Jurty_na_stepie_pomi%C4%99dzy_U%C5%82an_Bator_a_Karakorum_03.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Jurty_na_stepie_pomi%C4%99dzy_U%C5%82an_Bator_a_Karakorum_03.JPG/330px-Jurty_na_stepie_pomi%C4%99dzy_U%C5%82an_Bator_a_Karakorum_03.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Jurty_na_stepie_pomi%C4%99dzy_U%C5%82an_Bator_a_Karakorum_03.JPG/440px-Jurty_na_stepie_pomi%C4%99dzy_U%C5%82an_Bator_a_Karakorum_03.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2941" data-file-height="1961" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Jurty_na_stepie_pomi%C4%99dzy_U%C5%82an_Bator_a_Karakorum_03.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Mongolian-Manchurian_grassland" class="mw-redirect" title="Mongolian-Manchurian grassland">Mongolian steppe</a></div></div></div>
<p>The name "Gobi" is a Mongol term for a desert steppe, which usually refers to a category of arid rangeland with insufficient vegetation to support <a href="/wiki/Marmot" title="Marmot">marmots</a> but with enough to support <a href="/wiki/Camel" title="Camel">camels</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_58-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-58">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Mongols distinguish Gobi from desert proper, although the distinction is not always apparent to outsiders unfamiliar with the Mongolian landscape.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_58-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-58">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gobi rangelands are fragile and easily destroyed by overgrazing, which results in expansion of the true desert, a stony waste where not even <a href="/wiki/Bactrian_camel" title="Bactrian camel">Bactrian camels</a> can survive.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_58-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-58">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The arid conditions in the Gobi are attributed to the <a href="/wiki/Rain_shadow_effect" class="mw-redirect" title="Rain shadow effect">rain shadow effect</a> caused by the Himalayas. Before the Himalayas were formed by the collision of the Indo-Australian plate with the Eurasian plate 10 million years ago, Mongolia was a flourishing habitat for major fauna but still somewhat arid and cold due to distance from sources of evaporation. Sea turtle and mollusk fossils have been found in the Gobi, apart from well-known dinosaur fossils. <a href="/wiki/Tadpole_shrimps" class="mw-redirect" title="Tadpole shrimps">Tadpole shrimps</a> (<i>Lepidurus mongolicus</i>) are still found in the Gobi today. The eastern part of Mongolia including the <a href="/wiki/Onon_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Onon River">Onon</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kherlen_River" title="Kherlen River">Kherlen</a> rivers and <a href="/wiki/Lake_Buir" class="mw-redirect" title="Lake Buir">Lake Buir</a> form part of the <a href="/wiki/Amur_river" class="mw-redirect" title="Amur river">Amur river</a> basin draining to the Pacific Ocean. It hosts some unique species like the Eastern brook lamprey, Daurian crayfish (<i>cambaroides dauricus</i>) and Daurian pearl oyster (<i>dahurinaia dahurica</i>) in the Onon/Kherlen rivers as well as Siberian prawn (<i>exopalaemon modestus</i>) in Lake Buir.
</p><p>Mongolia had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 9.36/10, ranking it sixth globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-61">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Mongolia" title="Demographics of Mongolia">Demographics of Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Chinggis_Square.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Chinggis_Square.jpg/220px-Chinggis_Square.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Chinggis_Square.jpg/330px-Chinggis_Square.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Chinggis_Square.jpg/440px-Chinggis_Square.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1800" data-file-height="1196" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Chinggis_Square.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Ulaanbaatar" title="Ulaanbaatar">Ulaanbaatar</a> is the capital and largest city of Mongolia</div></div></div>
