<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Yurt_in_Ulan_Bator.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Yurt_in_Ulan_Bator.JPG/220px-Yurt_in_Ulan_Bator.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Yurt_in_Ulan_Bator.JPG/330px-Yurt_in_Ulan_Bator.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Yurt_in_Ulan_Bator.JPG/440px-Yurt_in_Ulan_Bator.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3328" data-file-height="1872" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Yurt_in_Ulan_Bator.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>In settlements, many families live in <a href="/wiki/Ger_district" title="Ger district"><i>ger</i> districts</a></div></div></div>
<p>Mongolia's total population as of January 2015 was estimated by the <a href="/wiki/U.S._Census_Bureau" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Census Bureau">U.S. Census Bureau</a><sup id="cite_ref-US_Census_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_Census-62">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="2015 population from a 2007 source is obviously incorrect (July 2021)">failed verification</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> to be 3,000,251 people, ranking around 121st in the world. But the <a href="/wiki/U.S._Department_of_State" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Department of State">U.S. Department of State</a> <a href="/wiki/Bureau_of_East_Asian_and_Pacific_Affairs" title="Bureau of East Asian and Pacific Affairs">Bureau of East Asian and Pacific Affairs</a> uses the United Nations (UN) estimations<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> instead of the U.S. Census Bureau estimations. <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Department_of_Economic_and_Social_Affairs" title="United Nations Department of Economic and Social Affairs">United Nations Department of Economic and Social Affairs</a> Population Division<sup id="cite_ref-UN_DESA_PD_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_DESA_PD-64">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> estimates Mongolia's total population (mid-2007) as 2,629,000 (11% less than the U.S. Census Bureau figure). UN estimates resemble those made by the Mongolian National Statistical Office (2,612,900, end of June 2007). Mongolia's population growth rate is estimated at 1.2% (2007 est.).<sup id="cite_ref-UN_DESA_PD_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_DESA_PD-64">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> About 59% of the total population is under age 30, 27% of whom are under 14. This relatively <a href="/wiki/Youth_in_Mongolia" title="Youth in Mongolia">young</a> and growing population has placed strains on Mongolia's economy.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The first census in the 20th century was carried out in 1918 and recorded a population of 647,500.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Since the end of socialism, Mongolia has experienced a decline of <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> (children per woman) that is steeper than in any other country in the world, according to recent UN estimations:<sup id="cite_ref-UN_DESA_PD_64-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_DESA_PD-64">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> in 1970–1975, fertility was estimated to be 7.33 children per woman, dropping to about 2.1 in 2000–2005.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> The decline ended and in 2005–2010, the estimated fertility value increased to 2.5 and stabilised afterwards at the rate of about 2.2–2.3 children per woman.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Ethnic Mongols account for about 95% of the population and consist of <a href="/wiki/Khalkha" class="mw-redirect" title="Khalkha">Khalkha</a> and other groups, all distinguished primarily by dialects of the Mongol language. The <a href="/wiki/Khalkha" class="mw-redirect" title="Khalkha">Khalkha</a> make up 86% of the ethnic Mongol population. The remaining 14% include <a href="/wiki/Oirats" title="Oirats">Oirats</a>, <a href="/wiki/Buryats" title="Buryats">Buryats</a> and others. <a href="/wiki/Turkic_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Turkic people">Turkic peoples</a> (<a href="/wiki/Kazakhs" title="Kazakhs">Kazakhs</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tuvans" title="Tuvans">Tuvans</a>) constitute 4.5% of Mongolia's population, and the rest are Russian, Chinese, Korean and American nationalities.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span></h3>
<p>The official language of Mongolia is <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_language" title="Mongolian language">Mongolian</a>, and is spoken by 95% of the population. A variety of <a href="/wiki/Dialect" title="Dialect">dialects</a> of <a href="/wiki/Oirat_language" title="Oirat language">Oirat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Buryat_language" title="Buryat language">Buryat</a> are spoken across the country, and there are also some speakers of <a href="/wiki/Khamnigan_Mongol" title="Khamnigan Mongol">Mongolic Khamnigan</a>. In the west of the country, <a href="/wiki/Kazakh_language" title="Kazakh language">Kazakh</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tuvan_language" title="Tuvan language">Tuvan</a>, both <a href="/wiki/Turkic_languages" title="Turkic languages">Turkic languages</a>, are also spoken. <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Sign_Language" title="Mongolian Sign Language">Mongolian Sign Language</a> is the principal language of the deaf community.
</p><p>Today, Mongolian is written using the <a href="/wiki/Cyrillic_alphabet" class="mw-redirect" title="Cyrillic alphabet">Cyrillic alphabet</a> in Mongolia, although in the past it was written using the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_script" title="Mongolian script">Mongolian script</a>. An official reintroduction of the old script was planned for 1994, but has not taken place as older generations encountered practical difficulties.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Schools are reintroducing the traditional alphabet.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> In March 2020, the Mongolian government announced plans to use both Cyrillic and the traditional Mongolian script in official documents by 2025.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Russian_language" title="Russian language">Russian</a> is the most frequently spoken foreign language in Mongolia, followed by <a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a>, although English has been gradually replacing Russian as the second language. <a href="/wiki/Korean_language" title="Korean language">Korean</a> has gained popularity as tens of thousands of <a href="/wiki/Mongolians_in_South_Korea" title="Mongolians in South Korea">Mongolians work in South Korea</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Mongolia" title="Religion in Mongolia">Religion in Mongolia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_religion_in_Mongolia" title="Freedom of religion in Mongolia">Freedom of religion in Mongolia</a></div>

<p>According to the 2010 National Census, among Mongolians aged 15 and above, 53% were <a href="/wiki/Buddhism_in_Mongolia" title="Buddhism in Mongolia">Buddhists</a>, while 39% were <a href="/wiki/Irreligion" title="Irreligion">non-religious</a>. 
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mongolian_shamanism" title="Mongolian shamanism">Mongolian shamanism</a> has been widely practised throughout the history of what is now Mongolia, with similar beliefs being common among the nomads of central Asia. They gradually gave way to <a href="/wiki/Tibetan_Buddhism" title="Tibetan Buddhism">Tibetan Buddhism</a>, but shamanism has left a mark on Mongolian religious culture, and it continues to be practiced. The Kazakhs of western Mongolia, some Mongols, and other Turkic peoples in the country traditionally adhere to <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Mongolia" title="Islam in Mongolia">Islam</a>.
</p><p>Throughout much of the 20th century, the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Republic" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Republic">communist</a> government repressed religious practices. It targeted the clergy of the Mongolian Buddhist Church, which had been tightly intertwined with the previous feudal government structures (e.g. from 1911 on, the head of the Church had also been the <a href="/wiki/Khan_(title)" title="Khan (title)">Khan</a> of the country).<sup id="cite_ref-eb_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eb-74">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> In the late 1930s, the regime, then led by <a href="/wiki/Khorloogiin_Choibalsan" title="Khorloogiin Choibalsan">Khorloogiin Choibalsan</a>, closed almost all of Mongolia's over 700 Buddhist monasteries and <a href="/wiki/Stalinist_repressions_in_Mongolia" title="Stalinist repressions in Mongolia">killed at least 30,000 people, of whom 18,000 were lamas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Thirty_thousand_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thirty_thousand-75">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> The number of <a href="/wiki/Buddhist_monk" class="mw-redirect" title="Buddhist monk">Buddhist monks</a> dropped from 100,000 in 1924 to 110 in 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-eb_74-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eb-74">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Amarbayasgalant_monastery_-_panoramio.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Amarbayasgalant_monastery_-_panoramio.jpg/220px-Amarbayasgalant_monastery_-_panoramio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Amarbayasgalant_monastery_-_panoramio.jpg/330px-Amarbayasgalant_monastery_-_panoramio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Amarbayasgalant_monastery_-_panoramio.jpg/440px-Amarbayasgalant_monastery_-_panoramio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="1080" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Amarbayasgalant_monastery_-_panoramio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Amarbayasgalant_Monastery" title="Amarbayasgalant Monastery">Amarbayasgalant Monastery</a></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Fall_of_communism" class="mw-redirect" title="Fall of communism">fall of communism</a> in 1991 restored public religious practice. <a href="/wiki/Tibetan_Buddhism" title="Tibetan Buddhism">Tibetan Buddhism</a>, which had been the predominant religion prior to the rise of communism, again rose to become the most widely practised religion in Mongolia. The end of religious repression in the 1990s also allowed for other religions to spread in the country. According to the Christian missionary group <a href="/wiki/Barnabas_Fund" title="Barnabas Fund">Barnabas Fund</a>, the number of Christians grew from just four in 1989 to around 40,000 as of 2008<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Mongolia&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>. In May 2013, <a href="/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints" title="The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints">The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints</a> (LDS Church) held a cultural program to celebrate twenty years of LDS Church history in Mongolia, with 10,900 members, and 16 church buildings in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-LDS_Members_Celebrate_20_Years_of_Church_in_Mongolia_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LDS_Members_Celebrate_20_Years_of_Church_in_Mongolia-76">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> There are some 1,000 Catholics in Mongolia and, in 2003, a missionary from the <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a> was named Mongolia's first <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a> bishop.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> In 2017 <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventists" class="mw-redirect" title="Seventh-day Adventists">Seventh-day Adventists</a> reported 2,700 members in six churches up from zero members in 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-adventistyearbook.org_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-adventistyearbook.org-78">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Mongolia" title="Politics of Mongolia">Politics of Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:President_Putin_meeting_deputies_of_the_Great_State_Hural-1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/President_Putin_meeting_deputies_of_the_Great_State_Hural-1.jpg/220px-President_Putin_meeting_deputies_of_the_Great_State_Hural-1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/President_Putin_meeting_deputies_of_the_Great_State_Hural-1.jpg/330px-President_Putin_meeting_deputies_of_the_Great_State_Hural-1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/President_Putin_meeting_deputies_of_the_Great_State_Hural-1.jpg/440px-President_Putin_meeting_deputies_of_the_Great_State_Hural-1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="334" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:President_Putin_meeting_deputies_of_the_Great_State_Hural-1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/State_Great_Khural" title="State Great Khural">State Great Khural</a> chamber in session</div></div></div>
<p>Mongolia is a <a href="/wiki/Semi-presidential_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Semi-presidential system">semi-presidential</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democratic</a> <a href="/wiki/Republic" title="Republic">republic</a> with a directly elected <a href="/wiki/President_of_Mongolia" title="President of Mongolia">President</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Draft_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Draft-79">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Dual_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dual-80">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-IDEA_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IDEA-5">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> The people also elect the deputies in the national assembly, the <a href="/wiki/State_Great_Khural" title="State Great Khural">State Great Khural</a>. The president appoints the prime minister, and nominates the cabinet on the proposal of the prime minister. The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Mongolia" title="Constitution of Mongolia">constitution of Mongolia</a> guarantees a number of freedoms, including full <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_expression" class="mw-redirect" title="Freedom of expression">freedom of expression</a> and <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_religion" title="Freedom of religion">religion</a>. Mongolia has a number of political parties; the largest are the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Party" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Party">Mongolian People's Party</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_(Mongolia)" title="Democratic Party (Mongolia)">Democratic Party</a>. The non-governmental organisation <a href="/wiki/Freedom_House" title="Freedom House">Freedom House</a> considers Mongolia to be free.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The People's Party – known as the People's Revolutionary Party between 1924 and 2010 – formed the government from 1921 to 1996 (in a <a href="/wiki/One-party_state" title="One-party state">one-party system</a> until 1990) and from 2000 to 2004. From 2004 to 2006, it was part of a coalition with the Democrats and two other parties, and after 2006 it was the dominant party in two other coalitions. The party initiated two changes of government from 2004 prior to losing power in the 2012 election. The Democrats were the dominant force in a ruling coalition between 1996 and 2000, and an almost-equal partner with the People's Revolutionary Party in a coalition between 2004 and 2006. An election of deputies to the national assembly on 28 June 2012 resulted in no party having an overall majority;<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> however, as the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_(Mongolia)" title="Democratic Party (Mongolia)">Democratic Party</a> won the largest number of seats,<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> its leader, <a href="/wiki/Norovyn_Altankhuyag" title="Norovyn Altankhuyag">Norovyn Altankhuyag</a>, was appointed <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Mongolia" title="Prime Minister of Mongolia">prime minister</a> on 10 August 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> In 2014, he was replaced by <a href="/wiki/Chimediin_Saikhanbileg" title="Chimediin Saikhanbileg">Chimediin Saikhanbileg</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Party" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Party">MPP</a> won a landslide victory in the <a href="/wiki/2016_Mongolian_legislative_election" title="2016 Mongolian legislative election">2016 elections</a> and the next <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Mongolia" title="Prime Minister of Mongolia">Prime Minister</a> was <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Party" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Party">MPP</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Ukhnaagiin_Kh%C3%BCrels%C3%BCkh" title="Ukhnaagiin Khürelsükh">Ukhnaagiin Khürelsükh</a>. In June 2020, MPP won a landslide victory in the <a href="/wiki/2020_Mongolian_legislative_election" title="2020 Mongolian legislative election">election</a>. It took 62 seats and the main opposition DP, 11 of the 76 seats. Before the elections the ruling party had redrawn the electoral map in a way that was beneficial for MPP.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
In January 2021, Prime Minister Ukhnaagiin Khürelsükh resigned after protests over the treatment of a coronavirus patient.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> On 27 January 2021, <a href="/wiki/Luvsannamsrain_Oyun-Erdene" title="Luvsannamsrain Oyun-Erdene">Luvsannamsrai Oyun-Erdene</a> of MPP became new prime minister. He represents a younger generation of leaders that had studied abroad.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Archers_in_Traditional_Mongolian_Outfits_Guide_Secretary_Kerry_as_He_Prepares_to_Shoot_an_Arrow_at_a_%22Mini-Nadaam%22_Outside_Ulaanbaatar_(27443915682).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" href="/wiki/File:Archers_in_Traditional_Mongolian_Outfits_Guide_Secretary_Kerry_as_He_Prepares_to_Shoot_an_Arrow_at_a_%22Mini-Nadaam%22_Outside_Ulaanbaatar_(27443915682).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mongolia's President <a href="/wiki/Tsakhia_Elbegdorj" class="mw-redirect" title="Tsakhia Elbegdorj">Tsakhia Elbegdorj</a> with U.S. Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/John_Kerry" title="John Kerry">John Kerry</a>, June 2016</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/President_of_Mongolia" title="President of Mongolia">President of Mongolia</a> is able to veto the laws made by parliament, appoint judges and justice of courts and appoint ambassadors. The parliament can override that veto by a two-thirds majority vote. Mongolia's constitution provides three requirements for taking office as president; the candidate must be a native-born Mongolian, be at least 45 years old, and have resided in Mongolia for five years before taking office. The president must also suspend their party membership. After defeating incumbent <a href="/wiki/Nambaryn_Enkhbayar" title="Nambaryn Enkhbayar">Nambaryn Enkhbayar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tsakhiagiin_Elbegdorj" title="Tsakhiagiin Elbegdorj">Tsakhiagiin Elbegdorj</a>, a two-time former prime minister and member of the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_(Mongolia)" title="Democratic Party (Mongolia)">Democratic Party</a>, was elected as president on 24 May 2009 and inaugurated on 18 June that year.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> The ruling <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Revolutionary_Party_(2010)" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Revolutionary Party (2010)">Mongolian People's Revolutionary Party (2010)</a> (<a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Revolutionary_Party_(2010)" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Revolutionary Party (2010)">MPRP</a>)nominated <a href="/wiki/Batbold_Sukhbaatar" class="mw-redirect" title="Batbold Sukhbaatar">Batbold Sukhbaatar</a> as new Prime Minister in October 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Elbegdorj was re-elected on 26 June 2013 and was inaugurated on 10 July 2013 for his second term as president.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> In June 2017, opposition <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_(Mongolia)" title="Democratic Party (Mongolia)">Democratic Party</a> candidate <a href="/wiki/Khaltmaagiin_Battulga" title="Khaltmaagiin Battulga">Khaltmaagiin Battulga</a> won the presidential <a href="/wiki/2017_Mongolian_presidential_election" title="2017 Mongolian presidential election">election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> He was inaugurated on 10 July 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In June 2021, former Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Ukhnaagiin_Kh%C3%BCrels%C3%BCkh" title="Ukhnaagiin Khürelsükh">Ukhnaa Khurelsukh</a>, the candidate of the ruling <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Party" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Party">Mongolian People's Party</a> (<a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Party" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Party">MPP</a>), became the country's sixth democratically elected president after winning the <a href="/wiki/2021_Mongolian_presidential_election" title="2021 Mongolian presidential election">presidential election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mongolia uses a <a href="/wiki/Unicameralism" title="Unicameralism">unicameral</a> <a href="/wiki/Legislature" title="Legislature">legislature</a>, the State Great Khural, with 76 seats, which is chaired by the Speaker of the House. Its members are directly elected, every four years, by popular vote.<sup id="cite_ref-IDEA_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IDEA-5">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Mongolia" title="Foreign relations of Mongolia">Foreign relations of Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_and_Khaltmaagiin_Battulga_(2017-09-07)_01.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Vladimir_Putin_and_Khaltmaagiin_Battulga_%282017-09-07%29_01.jpg/220px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Khaltmaagiin_Battulga_%282017-09-07%29_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="136" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Vladimir_Putin_and_Khaltmaagiin_Battulga_%282017-09-07%29_01.jpg/330px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Khaltmaagiin_Battulga_%282017-09-07%29_01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Vladimir_Putin_and_Khaltmaagiin_Battulga_%282017-09-07%29_01.jpg/440px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Khaltmaagiin_Battulga_%282017-09-07%29_01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1880" data-file-height="1160" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_and_Khaltmaagiin_Battulga_(2017-09-07)_01.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mongolia's President <a href="/wiki/Khaltmaagiin_Battulga" title="Khaltmaagiin Battulga">Khaltmaagiin Battulga</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a> in <a href="/wiki/Vladivostok" title="Vladivostok">Vladivostok</a>, September 2017</div></div></div>
<p>Mongolia's foreign relations traditionally focus on its two large neighbors, <a href="/wiki/Russia" title="Russia">Russia</a> and the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Republic_of_China" class="mw-redirect" title="People&#39;s Republic of China">People's Republic of China</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Mongolia is economically dependent on these countries: China receives 90% of Mongolia's exports by value and accounts for 60% of its foreign trade, while Russia supplies 90% of Mongolia's energy requirements.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Mongolia's most important trading partner is China, and the government has been trying to stay out of the current US-China confrontation.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It has begun seeking positive relations with a wider range of other nations especially in cultural and economic matters, focusing on encouraging foreign investments and trade.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Mongolia has been pursuing a third-neighbor foreign policy since early 1990s to build deeper relations and partnerships with countries beyond its two neighbors.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Embassies">Embassies</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_Mongolia" title="List of diplomatic missions of Mongolia">List of diplomatic missions of Mongolia</a></div>
<p>Mongolia maintains many <a href="/wiki/Diplomatic_missions" class="mw-redirect" title="Diplomatic missions">diplomatic missions</a> in other countries and has <a href="/wiki/Embassies" class="mw-redirect" title="Embassies">embassies</a> in the following world capitals:<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Armed_Forces" title="Mongolian Armed Forces">Mongolian Armed Forces</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vostok-2018_military_manoeuvres_(2018-09-13)_51.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Vostok-2018_military_manoeuvres_%282018-09-13%29_51.jpg/220px-Vostok-2018_military_manoeuvres_%282018-09-13%29_51.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="136" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Vostok-2018_military_manoeuvres_%282018-09-13%29_51.jpg/330px-Vostok-2018_military_manoeuvres_%282018-09-13%29_51.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Vostok-2018_military_manoeuvres_%282018-09-13%29_51.jpg/440px-Vostok-2018_military_manoeuvres_%282018-09-13%29_51.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1880" data-file-height="1160" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vostok-2018_military_manoeuvres_(2018-09-13)_51.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mongolian, Chinese and Russian national flags set on armored vehicles during the large-scale military exercise <a href="/wiki/Vostok_2018" title="Vostok 2018">Vostok 2018</a> in Eastern Siberia</div></div></div>
<p>Mongolia supported the <a href="/wiki/2003_invasion_of_Iraq" title="2003 invasion of Iraq">2003 invasion of Iraq</a>, and has sent several successive contingents of 103 to 180 troops each to <a href="/wiki/Iraq" title="Iraq">Iraq</a>. About 130 troops were deployed to <a href="/wiki/Afghanistan" title="Afghanistan">Afghanistan</a>. 200 Mongolian troops are serving in <a href="/wiki/Sierra_Leone" title="Sierra Leone">Sierra Leone</a> on a UN mandate to protect the UN's <a href="/wiki/Special_Court_for_Sierra_Leone" title="Special Court for Sierra Leone">special court</a> set up there, and in July 2009, Mongolia decided to send a <a href="/wiki/Battalion" title="Battalion">battalion</a> to <a href="/wiki/Chad" title="Chad">Chad</a> in support of <a href="/wiki/MINURCAT" class="mw-redirect" title="MINURCAT">MINURCAT</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From 2005 to 2006, about 40 troops were deployed with the Belgian and Luxembourg contingents in <a href="/wiki/Kosovo" title="Kosovo">Kosovo</a>. On 21 November 2005, <a href="/wiki/George_W._Bush" title="George W. Bush">George W. Bush</a> became the first-ever sitting U.S. president to visit Mongolia.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> In 2004, under Bulgarian chairmanship, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe</a> (OSCE) invited Mongolia as its newest Asian partner.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Legal_system">Legal system</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Judiciary_of_Mongolia" title="Judiciary of Mongolia">Judiciary of Mongolia</a></div>
<p>The <b>judiciary of Mongolia</b> is made of a three-tiered <a href="/wiki/Court" title="Court">court</a> system: <a href="/wiki/Trial_court" title="Trial court">first instance</a> courts in each <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Mongolia" title="Districts of Mongolia">provincial district</a> and each <a href="/wiki/D%C3%BC%C3%BCrg%C3%BC%C3%BCd_of_Mongolia" class="mw-redirect" title="Düürgüüd of Mongolia">Ulaanbaatar district</a>; <a href="/wiki/Appellate_court" title="Appellate court">appellate</a> courts for each province and also the Capital Ulaanbaatar; and the court of last resort (for non-constitutional matters) at the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Mongolia" title="Supreme Court of Mongolia">Supreme Court of Mongolia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> For questions of constitutional law there is a separate <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_court" title="Constitutional court">constitutional court</a>.
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/Judicial_General_Council_of_Mongolia" title="Judicial General Council of Mongolia">Judicial General Council</a> (JGC) nominates judges which must then be confirmed by the <a href="/wiki/State_Great_Khural" title="State Great Khural">parliament</a> and appointed by the President.
</p><p>Arbitration centres provide <a href="/wiki/Alternative_dispute_resolution" title="Alternative dispute resolution">alternative dispute resolution</a> options for commercial and other disputes.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span></h3>

<p>Mongolia is divided into 21 <a href="/wiki/Provinces_of_Mongolia" title="Provinces of Mongolia">provinces (aimags)</a> and subdivided into 331 <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Mongolia" title="Districts of Mongolia">districts (sums)</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> The capital <a href="/wiki/Ulaanbaatar" title="Ulaanbaatar">Ulaanbaatar</a> is administrated separately as a <a href="/wiki/Provinces_of_Mongolia" title="Provinces of Mongolia">capital city (municipality)</a> with provincial status. The <i>aimags</i> are:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r998391716"/><div class="div-col" style="column-width: 10em;">
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Arkhangai_Province" title="Arkhangai Province">Arkhangai</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Bayan-%C3%96lgii_Province" title="Bayan-Ölgii Province">Bayan-Ölgii</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Bayankhongor_Province" title="Bayankhongor Province">Bayankhongor</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Bulgan_Province" title="Bulgan Province">Bulgan</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Darkhan-Uul_Province" title="Darkhan-Uul Province">Darkhan-Uul</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Dornod_Province" title="Dornod Province">Dornod</a></li>

<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Mongolia" title="List of cities in Mongolia">List of cities in Mongolia</a></div>
<p>About 40% of the population lives in <a href="/wiki/Ulaanbaatar" title="Ulaanbaatar">Ulaanbaatar (Ulan Bator)</a>, and in 2002 a further 23% lived in <a href="/wiki/Darkhan_(city)" title="Darkhan (city)">Darkhan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Erdenet" title="Erdenet">Erdenet</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Aimags_of_Mongolia" class="mw-redirect" title="Aimags of Mongolia">aimag</a> centers and <a href="/wiki/Sums_of_Mongolia" class="mw-redirect" title="Sums of Mongolia">sum</a>-level permanent settlements.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> Another share of the population lives in the sum centers.
</p>
