<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Mongolia" title="Economy of Mongolia">Economy of Mongolia</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="42" height="34" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/63px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/84px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="290" /></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section needs to be <b>updated</b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">August 2016</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mongolia.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mongolia.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mongolia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mongolia.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mongolia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mongolia.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mongolia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mongolia.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Historical development of real GDP per capita in Mongolia</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolia_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Mongolia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Mongolia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Mongolia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Mongolia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Mongolia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Mongolia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolia_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Mongolia exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:UB_downtown.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/UB_downtown.jpg/220px-UB_downtown.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/UB_downtown.jpg/330px-UB_downtown.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/UB_downtown.jpg/440px-UB_downtown.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1800" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:UB_downtown.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>View of Ulaanbaatar with the <a href="/wiki/Blue_Sky_Tower" title="Blue Sky Tower">Blue Sky Tower</a></div></div></div>
<p>Economic activity in Mongolia has long been based on herding and agriculture, although development of extensive mineral deposits of copper, coal, <a href="/wiki/Molybdenum" title="Molybdenum">molybdenum</a>, tin, <a href="/wiki/Tungsten" title="Tungsten">tungsten</a> and gold have emerged as a driver of industrial production.<sup id="cite_ref-stategov_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stategov-105">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Besides mining (21.8% of GDP) and agriculture (16% of GDP), dominant industries in the composition of GDP are wholesale and retail trade and service, transportation and storage, and real estate activities.<sup id="cite_ref-stategov_105-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stategov-105">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Informal_economy" title="Informal economy">informal economy</a> is estimated to be at least one-third the size of the official economy.<sup id="cite_ref-stategov_105-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stategov-105">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> As of 2006<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Mongolia&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 68.4% of Mongolia's exports went to the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Republic_of_China" class="mw-redirect" title="People&#39;s Republic of China">PRC</a>, and the PRC supplied 29.8% of Mongolia's imports.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mongolia is ranked as a lower-middle-income economy by the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Some 22.4% of the population lives on less than <a href="/wiki/United_States_dollar" title="United States dollar">US$</a>1.25 a day.<sup id="cite_ref-HDI-2011_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HDI-2011-108">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, GDP per capita was $3,100.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_6-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Despite growth, the proportion of the population below the poverty line was estimated to be 35.6% in 1998, 36.1% in 2002–2003, and 32.2% in 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-Yearbook2007_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Yearbook2007-109">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Because of a boom in the mining sector, Mongolia had high growth rates in 2007 and 2008 (9.9% and 8.9%, respectively).<sup id="cite_ref-stategov_105-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stategov-105">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> In 2009, sharp drops in commodity prices and the effects of the global financial crisis caused the local currency to drop 40% against the U.S. dollar. Two of the 16 commercial banks were taken into receivership.<sup id="cite_ref-stategov_105-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stategov-105">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, GDP growth was expected to reach 16.4%. However, inflation continued to erode GDP gains, with an average rate of 12.6% expected at the end of 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-stategov_105-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stategov-105">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Although GDP has risen steadily since 2002 at the rate of 7.5% in an official 2006 estimate, the state is still working to overcome a sizable trade deficit. The <i>Economist</i> predicted this trade deficit of 14% of Mongolia's GDP would transform into a surplus in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-econ-mam_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-econ-mam-110">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mongolia was never listed among the <a href="/wiki/Emerging_market" title="Emerging market">emerging market</a> countries until February 2011 when <a href="/wiki/Citigroup" title="Citigroup">Citigroup</a> analysts determined Mongolia to be one of the "<a href="/wiki/3G_(countries)" class="mw-redirect" title="3G (countries)">global growth generating</a>" countries, which are countries with the most promising growth prospects for 2010–2050.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Stock_Exchange" title="Mongolian Stock Exchange">Mongolian Stock Exchange</a>, established in 1991 in Ulaanbaatar, is among the world's smallest <a href="/wiki/Stock_exchange" title="Stock exchange">stock exchanges</a> by <a href="/wiki/Market_capitalisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Market capitalisation">market capitalisation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Jeffs_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jeffs-112">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-IHT_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IHT-113">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, it had 336 companies listed with a total market capitalization of US$2&#160;billion after quadrupling from US$406&#160;million in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Mongolia made a significant improvement in the ease of doing business in 2012, ranking 76th compared with 88th the previous year in the "Doing Business" report by the <a href="/wiki/International_Finance_Corporation" title="International Finance Corporation">International Finance Corporation</a> (IFC).<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mineral_industry">Mineral industry</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mining_in_Mongolia" title="Mining in Mongolia">Mining in Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Oyu_Tolgoi_23.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Oyu_Tolgoi_23.JPG/220px-Oyu_Tolgoi_23.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Oyu_Tolgoi_23.JPG/330px-Oyu_Tolgoi_23.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Oyu_Tolgoi_23.JPG/440px-Oyu_Tolgoi_23.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3328" data-file-height="1872" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Oyu_Tolgoi_23.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Oyu_Tolgoi" class="mw-redirect" title="Oyu Tolgoi">Oyu Tolgoi</a> employs 18,000 workers and expects to be producing 450,000 tonnes of copper a year by 2020<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>Minerals represent more than 80% of Mongolia's exports, a proportion expected to eventually rise to 95%. Fiscal revenues from mining represented 21% of government income in 2010 and rose to 24% in 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> About 3,000 mining licences have been issued.<sup id="cite_ref-econ-mam_110-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-econ-mam-110">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> Mining continues to rise as a major industry of Mongolia as evidenced by the number of Chinese, Russian and Canadian firms starting mining businesses in Mongolia.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_6-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2009, the government negotiated an "investment agreement" with <a href="/wiki/Rio_Tinto_Group" class="mw-redirect" title="Rio Tinto Group">Rio Tinto</a> and <a href="/wiki/Turquoise_Hill_Resources" title="Turquoise Hill Resources">Ivanhoe Mines</a> to develop the <a href="/wiki/Oyu_Tolgoi" class="mw-redirect" title="Oyu Tolgoi">Oyu Tolgoi</a> copper and gold deposit,<sup id="cite_ref-stategov_105-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stategov-105">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> the biggest foreign-investment project in Mongolia, expected to account for one-third of Mongolia's GDP by 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-econ-mam_110-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-econ-mam-110">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> In March 2011, six big mining companies prepared to bid for the <a href="/wiki/Tavan_Tolgoi" title="Tavan Tolgoi">Tavan Tolgoi</a> area, the world's largest untapped coal deposit. According to Erdenes MGL, the government body in-charge of Tavan Tolgoi, <a href="/wiki/ArcelorMittal" title="ArcelorMittal">ArcelorMittal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vale_(mining_company)" class="mw-redirect" title="Vale (mining company)">Vale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Xstrata" title="Xstrata">Xstrata</a>, U.S. coal miner <a href="/wiki/Peabody_Energy" title="Peabody Energy">Peabody</a>, a consortium of Chinese energy firm <a href="/wiki/Shenhua_Group" title="Shenhua Group">Shenhua</a> and Japan's <a href="/wiki/Mitsui_%26_Co" class="mw-redirect" title="Mitsui &amp; Co">Mitsui &amp; Co</a>, and a separate consortium of Japanese, South Korean and Russian firms are the preferred bidders.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2022, Mongolia built and launched a 233-km direct rail link to China, which is a milestone in Mongolia’s plan to become China’s leading supplier of high-quality coal from the Tavan Tolgoi mine, which has more than six billion tonnes of coal reserves.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Agriculture">Agriculture</span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066933788"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Agriculture_in_Mongolia" title="Agriculture in Mongolia">Agriculture in Mongolia</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Agriculture_in_Mongolia&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="42" height="34" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/63px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/84px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="290" /></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This article needs to be <b>updated</b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">April 2010</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:342px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolia_Landscape.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Mongolia_Landscape.jpg/340px-Mongolia_Landscape.jpg" decoding="async" width="340" height="453" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Mongolia_Landscape.jpg/510px-Mongolia_Landscape.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Mongolia_Landscape.jpg/680px-Mongolia_Landscape.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="1600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolia_Landscape.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mongolian landscape</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Agriculture_in_Mongolia" title="Agriculture in Mongolia">Agriculture in Mongolia</a> constitutes over 10% of Mongolia's annual <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">Gross domestic product</a> and employs one-third of the labor force.<sup id="cite_ref-Agriculture_in_Mongolia_auto_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Agriculture_in_Mongolia_auto-121">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> However, the high altitude, extreme fluctuation in temperature, long winters, and low precipitation provides limited potential for agricultural development. The growing season is only 95 – 110 days.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Because of Mongolia's harsh climate, it is unsuited to most cultivation. 
</p><p>The agriculture sector therefore remains heavily focused on nomadic animal husbandry with 75% of the land allocated to pasture, and cropping only employing 3% of the population. In 2002, about 30% of all households in Mongolia lived from breeding livestock.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Most herders in Mongolia follow a pattern of <a href="/wiki/Nomads" class="mw-redirect" title="Nomads">nomadic</a> or semi-nomadic <a href="/wiki/Pastoralism" title="Pastoralism">pastoralism</a>.
</p>
Crops produced in Mongolia include <a href="/wiki/Maize" title="Maize">corn</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wheat" title="Wheat">wheat</a>, <a href="/wiki/Barley" title="Barley">barley</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Potato" title="Potato">potatoes</a>.  Animals raised commercially in Mongolia include <a href="/wiki/Sheep" title="Sheep">sheep</a>, <a href="/wiki/Goat" title="Goat">goats</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cow" class="mw-redirect" title="Cow">cattle</a>, <a href="/wiki/Horse" title="Horse">horses</a>, <a href="/wiki/Camel" title="Camel">camels</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pig" title="Pig">pigs</a>. They are raised primarily for their meat, although goats are valued for their hair which can be used to produce <a href="/wiki/Cashmere_wool" title="Cashmere wool">cashmere</a>. Due to the severe 2009–2010 winter, Mongolia lost 9.7&#160;million animals, or 22% of total livestock. This immediately affected meat prices, which increased twofold; the GDP dropped 1.6% in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-Agriculture_in_Mongolia_stategov_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Agriculture_in_Mongolia_stategov-124">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Communications">Communications</span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066933788"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Mongolia" title="Telecommunications in Mongolia">Telecommunications in Mongolia</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Telecommunications_in_Mongolia&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<p>Telecommunications in Mongolia face unique challenges. As the least densely populated country in the world, with a significant portion of the population living a nomadic lifestyle, it has been difficult for many traditional <a href="/wiki/Information_and_communication_technology" class="mw-redirect" title="Information and communication technology">information and communication technology</a> (ICT) companies to make headway into Mongolian society. With almost half the population clustered in the capital of <a href="/wiki/Ulaanbaatar" title="Ulaanbaatar">Ulaanbaatar</a>, most landline technologies are deployed there. Wireless technologies have had greater success in rural areas.
</p><p>Mobile phones are common, with <a href="/wiki/Provinces_of_Mongolia" title="Provinces of Mongolia">provincial capitals</a> all having <a href="/wiki/4G" title="4G">4G</a> access. <a href="/wiki/Wireless_local_loop" title="Wireless local loop">Wireless local loop</a> is another technology that has helped Mongolia increase accessibility to telecommunications and bypass fixed-line infrastructure.
</p><p>For Internet, Mongolia relies on <a href="/wiki/Optical_fibers" class="mw-redirect" title="Optical fibers">fiber optic</a> communications with its Chinese and Russian neighbors.
</p>
In 2005, Mongolia's state-run radio and TV provider converted to a public service provider. Private radio and TV broadcasters, multi-channel satellite, and <a href="/wiki/Cable_television" title="Cable television">cable TV</a> providers are also available.<sup id="cite_ref-Telecommunications_in_Mongolia_cia-fb-2013_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Telecommunications_in_Mongolia_cia-fb-2013-125">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<p>Postal services are provided by state-owned <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Post" title="Mongol Post">Mongol Post</a> and 54 other licensed operators.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy">Energy</span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066933788"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Energy_in_Mongolia" title="Energy in Mongolia">Energy in Mongolia</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Energy_in_Mongolia&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
Mongolia had a total <a href="/wiki/Primary_energy" title="Primary energy">primary energy</a> supply (<a href="/wiki/TPES" class="mw-redirect" title="TPES">TPES</a>) of 6.66 <a href="/wiki/Tonne_of_oil_equivalent" title="Tonne of oil equivalent">Mtoe</a> in 2019. Electricity consumption was 7.71 <a href="/wiki/TWh" class="mw-redirect" title="TWh">TWh</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Energy_in_Mongolia_iea2019_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Energy_in_Mongolia_iea2019-127">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> Mongolia is a big producer of <a href="/wiki/Coal" title="Coal">coal</a>, which is mostly exported.<sup id="cite_ref-Energy_in_Mongolia_SourceWatch_2018_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Energy_in_Mongolia_SourceWatch_2018-128">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Domestic consumption of coal accounts for about 70% of Mongolia's primary energy and makes up most of the electricity generation, accounting for about 87% of the domestic electricity production in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-Energy_in_Mongolia_iea2019_127-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Energy_in_Mongolia_iea2019-127">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transportation">Transportation</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Mongolia" title="Transport in Mongolia">Transport in Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Zamyn_Uud_traders.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Zamyn_Uud_traders.jpg/220px-Zamyn_Uud_traders.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="98" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Zamyn_Uud_traders.jpg/330px-Zamyn_Uud_traders.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Zamyn_Uud_traders.jpg/440px-Zamyn_Uud_traders.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="893" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Zamyn_Uud_traders.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Train in <a href="/wiki/Zamyn-%C3%9C%C3%BCd" title="Zamyn-Üüd">Zamyn-Üüd</a> station in <a href="/wiki/Dornogovi_Province" title="Dornogovi Province">Dornogovi aimag</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rider_in_Mongolia,_2012.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Rider_in_Mongolia%2C_2012.jpg/220px-Rider_in_Mongolia%2C_2012.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="117" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Rider_in_Mongolia%2C_2012.jpg/330px-Rider_in_Mongolia%2C_2012.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Rider_in_Mongolia%2C_2012.jpg/440px-Rider_in_Mongolia%2C_2012.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5759" data-file-height="3061" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rider_in_Mongolia,_2012.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>While the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_horse" title="Mongolian horse">Mongolian horse</a> continues to be revered as the national symbol, they are rapidly being replaced by motorized vehicles.</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%91%D0%B0%D0%B9%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BB_%D0%B8_%D0%A5%D1%83%D0%B1%D1%81%D1%83%D0%B3%D1%83%D0%BB_332.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/%D0%91%D0%B0%D0%B9%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BB_%D0%B8_%D0%A5%D1%83%D0%B1%D1%81%D1%83%D0%B3%D1%83%D0%BB_332.jpg/220px-%D0%91%D0%B0%D0%B9%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BB_%D0%B8_%D0%A5%D1%83%D0%B1%D1%81%D1%83%D0%B3%D1%83%D0%BB_332.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/%D0%91%D0%B0%D0%B9%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BB_%D0%B8_%D0%A5%D1%83%D0%B1%D1%81%D1%83%D0%B3%D1%83%D0%BB_332.jpg/330px-%D0%91%D0%B0%D0%B9%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BB_%D0%B8_%D0%A5%D1%83%D0%B1%D1%81%D1%83%D0%B3%D1%83%D0%BB_332.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/%D0%91%D0%B0%D0%B9%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BB_%D0%B8_%D0%A5%D1%83%D0%B1%D1%81%D1%83%D0%B3%D1%83%D0%BB_332.jpg/440px-%D0%91%D0%B0%D0%B9%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BB_%D0%B8_%D0%A5%D1%83%D0%B1%D1%81%D1%83%D0%B3%D1%83%D0%BB_332.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%91%D0%B0%D0%B9%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BB_%D0%B8_%D0%A5%D1%83%D0%B1%D1%81%D1%83%D0%B3%D1%83%D0%BB_332.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mongolian ferry <i>Sukhbaatar</i> on <a href="/wiki/Lake_Khovsgol" class="mw-redirect" title="Lake Khovsgol">Lake Khovsgol</a> in Khovsgol Province</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Trans-Mongolian_Railway" title="Trans-Mongolian Railway">Trans-Mongolian Railway</a> is the main rail link between Mongolia and its neighbors. It begins at the <a href="/wiki/Trans-Siberian_Railway" title="Trans-Siberian Railway">Trans-Siberian Railway</a> in Russia at the town of <a href="/wiki/Ulan-Ude" title="Ulan-Ude">Ulan-Ude</a>, crosses into Mongolia, runs through Ulaanbaatar, then passes into China at <a href="/wiki/Erenhot" title="Erenhot">Erenhot</a> where it joins the Chinese railway system. A separate railroad link connects the eastern city of <a href="/wiki/Choibalsan_(city)" title="Choibalsan (city)">Choibalsan</a> with the Trans-Siberian Railway. However, that link is closed to passengers after the Mongolian town of <a href="/wiki/Chuluunkhoroot" title="Chuluunkhoroot">Chuluunkhoroot</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> Mongolia also has a 233&#160;km-long cargo rail link from the Tavan Tolgoi coal mine to Chinese border.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mongolia has a number of domestic airports, with some of them having international status. However, the main international airport is <a href="/wiki/Chinggis_Khaan_International_Airport" title="Chinggis Khaan International Airport">Chinggis Khaan International Airport</a>, located approximately 52&#160;km (32&#160;mi) south of the capital Ulaanbaatar. Direct flight connections exist between Mongolia and South Korea, China, Thailand, Hong Kong, Japan, Russia, Germany, and Turkey. <a href="/wiki/MIAT_Mongolian_Airlines" title="MIAT Mongolian Airlines">MIAT Mongolian Airlines</a> is Mongolia's national air carrier, operating international flights, while air carriers such as Aero Mongolia and Hunnu Airlines serve domestic and short international routes.
</p><p>Many overland roads in Mongolia are only gravel roads or simple cross-country tracks. There are paved roads from Ulaanbaatar to the Russian and Chinese borders, from Ulaanbaatar east- and westward (the so-called Millennium Road), and from Darkhan to <a href="/wiki/Bulgan_(city)" title="Bulgan (city)">Bulgan</a>. A number of road construction projects are currently underway. Mongolia has 4,800&#160;km (3,000&#160;mi) of paved roads, with 1,800&#160;km (1,100&#160;mi) of that total completed in 2013 alone.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Mongolia" title="Education in Mongolia">Education in Mongolia</a></div>
<p>During the state socialist period, education was one of the areas of significant achievement in Mongolia. Before the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Republic" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Republic">People's Republic</a>, literacy rates were below one percent. By 1952, illiteracy was virtually eliminated,<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> in part through the use of seasonal boarding schools for children of nomadic families. Funding to these boarding schools was cut in the 1990s, contributing to slightly increased illiteracy.
</p><p>Primary and secondary education formerly lasted ten years, but was expanded to eleven years. Since the 2008–2009 school year, new first-graders are using the 12-year system, with a full transition to the 12-year system in the 2019–2020 school year.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2006<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Mongolia&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, English is taught in all secondary schools across Mongolia, beginning in fourth grade.
</p><p>Mongolian national universities are all spin-offs from the <a href="/wiki/National_University_of_Mongolia" title="National University of Mongolia">National University of Mongolia</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_University_of_Science_and_Technology" title="Mongolian University of Science and Technology">Mongolian University of Science and Technology</a>. Almost three in five Mongolian youths now enroll in university. There was a six-fold increase in students between 1993 and 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Mongolia was ranked 58th in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2021, down from 53rd in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066933788"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Mongolia" title="Health in Mongolia">Health in Mongolia</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Health_in_Mongolia&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Life_expectancy_by_WBG_-Mongolia.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Life_expectancy_by_WBG_-Mongolia.png/220px-Life_expectancy_by_WBG_-Mongolia.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="128" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Life_expectancy_by_WBG_-Mongolia.png/330px-Life_expectancy_by_WBG_-Mongolia.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Life_expectancy_by_WBG_-Mongolia.png/440px-Life_expectancy_by_WBG_-Mongolia.png 2x" data-file-width="1162" data-file-height="675" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Life_expectancy_by_WBG_-Mongolia.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Life expectancy in Mongolia</div></div></div>
<p>Modern Mongolia inherited a relatively good healthcare system from its socialist period, a world bank report from 2007 notes "despite its low per capita income, Mongolia has relatively strong health indicators; a reflection of the important health gains achieved during the socialist period." On average Mongolia's infant mortality rate is less than half of that of similarly economically developed countries, its under-five mortality rate and life expectancy are all better on average than other nations with similar GDP per capita.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 1990, key health indicators in Mongolia like life expectancy and <a href="/wiki/Infant_mortality" title="Infant mortality">infant</a> and <a href="/wiki/Child_mortality" title="Child mortality">child mortality</a> have steadily improved, both due to social changes and to improvement in the health sector. <a href="/wiki/Echinococcosis" title="Echinococcosis">Echinococcosis</a> was one of the commonest surgical diagnoses in the 1960s, but now has been greatly reduced.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> Yet, adult health deteriorated during the 1990s and the first decade of the 21st century and mortality rates increased significantly.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>  Smallpox, typhus, plague, poliomyelitis, and diphtheria were eradicated by 1981.  The <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Red_Cross_Society" title="Mongolian Red Cross Society">Mongolian Red Cross Society</a> focuses on preventive work. The <a href="/wiki/Confederation_of_Mongolian_Trade_Unions" title="Confederation of Mongolian Trade Unions">Confederation of Mongolian Trade Unions</a> established a network of sanatoriums.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Serious problems remain, especially in the countryside.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> According to a 2011 study by the <a href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization" title="World Health Organization">World Health Organization</a>, Mongolia's capital city, <a href="/wiki/Ulaanbaatar" title="Ulaanbaatar">Ulaanbaatar</a>, has the second highest level of fine particle pollution of any city in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> Poor air quality is also the largest occupational hazard, as over two-thirds of occupational disease in Mongolia is dust induced chronic <a href="/wiki/Bronchitis" title="Bronchitis">bronchitis</a> or <a href="/wiki/Pneumoconiosis" title="Pneumoconiosis">pneumoconiosis</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
Average childbirth (<a href="/wiki/Fertility_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Fertility rate">fertility rate</a>) is around 2.25<sup id="cite_ref-Health_in_Mongolia_US_Census_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Health_in_Mongolia_US_Census-146">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>–1.87<sup id="cite_ref-Health_in_Mongolia_UN_DESA_PD_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Health_in_Mongolia_UN_DESA_PD-147">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> per woman (2007) and average <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">life expectancy</a> is 68.5 years (2011).<sup id="cite_ref-Health_in_Mongolia_HDI-2011_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Health_in_Mongolia_HDI-2011-148">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Infant mortality is at 1.9%<sup id="cite_ref-Health_in_Mongolia_NMH_Yearbook_2006_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Health_in_Mongolia_NMH_Yearbook_2006-149">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> to 4%<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> and child mortality is at 4.3%.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Mongolia" title="Culture of Mongolia">Culture of Mongolia</a></div>
<p>The symbol in the left bar of the national flag is a Buddhist icon called <a href="/wiki/Soyombo_symbol" title="Soyombo symbol">Soyombo</a>. It represents the sun, moon, stars, and heavens per standard cosmological symbology abstracted from that seen in traditional <a href="/wiki/Thangka" title="Thangka">thangka</a> paintings.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Visual_arts">Visual arts</span></h3>
<p>Before the 20th century, most works of the fine arts in Mongolia had a religious function, and therefore Mongolian fine arts were heavily influenced by religious texts.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Thangkas" class="mw-redirect" title="Thangkas">Thangkas</a> were usually painted or made in <a href="/wiki/Appliqu%C3%A9" title="Appliqué">appliqué</a> technique. Bronze sculptures usually showed Buddhist deities. A number of great works are attributed to the first <a href="/wiki/Jebtsundamba_Khutuktu" title="Jebtsundamba Khutuktu">Jebtsundamba Khutuktu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zanabazar" title="Zanabazar">Zanabazar</a>.
</p><p>In the late 19th century, painters like <a href="/wiki/Marzan_Sharav" title="Marzan Sharav">"Marzan" Sharav</a> turned to more realistic painting styles. Under the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_People%27s_Republic" title="Mongolian People&#39;s Republic">Mongolian People's Republic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Socialist_realism" title="Socialist realism">socialist realism</a> was the dominant painting style,<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> however traditional <i>thangka</i>-like paintings dealing with secular, nationalist themes were also popular, a genre known as "<a href="/wiki/Mongol_zurag" title="Mongol zurag">Mongol <i>zurag</i></a>".
</p><p>Among the first attempts to introduce <a href="/wiki/Modernism" title="Modernism">modernism</a> into the fine arts of Mongolia was the painting <i>Ehiin setgel</i> (<i>Mother's love</i>) created by Tsevegjav in the 1960s. The artist was purged as his work was censored.
</p><p>All forms of fine arts flourished only after "<a href="/wiki/Perestroika" title="Perestroika">Perestroika</a>" in the late 1980s. Otgonbayar Ershuu is arguably one of the most well-known Mongolian modern artists in the Western world, he was portrayed in the film "ZURAG" by Tobias Wulff.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Architecture">Architecture</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Architecture_of_Mongolia" title="Architecture of Mongolia">Architecture of Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Gurvger.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Gurvger.jpg/220px-Gurvger.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="132" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Gurvger.jpg/330px-Gurvger.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Gurvger.jpg/440px-Gurvger.jpg 2x" data-file-width="648" data-file-height="390" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Gurvger.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <a href="/wiki/Yurt" title="Yurt"><i>ger</i></a> in front of the <a href="/wiki/Gurvan_Saikhan_Uul" class="mw-redirect" title="Gurvan Saikhan Uul">Gurvan Saikhan Mountains</a></div></div></div>
<p>The traditional Mongolian dwelling is known as a <i>ger</i>. In the past it was known by the Russian term <i>yurt</i>, but this has been changing as the Mongolian term becomes better known in English-speaking countries. According to Mongolian artist and art critic <a href="/w/index.php?title=N._Chultem&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="N. Chultem (page does not exist)">N. Chultem</a>, the <i>ger</i> was the basis for development of traditional Mongolian architecture. In the 16th and 17th centuries, lamaseries were built throughout the country. Many of them started as <i>ger</i>-temples. When they needed to be enlarged to accommodate the growing number of worshippers, the Mongolian architects used structures with 6 and 12 angles<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag may need clarification or removal of jargon. (April 2011)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> with pyramidal roofs to approximate to the round shape of a <i>ger</i>. Further enlargement led to a quadratic shape of the temples. The roofs were made in the shape of marquées.<sup id="cite_ref-Chultem_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chultem-155">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> The trellis walls, roof poles and layers of felt were replaced by stone, brick, beams and planks, and became permanent.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Chultem distinguished three styles in traditional Mongolian architecture: Mongolian, <a href="/wiki/Tibetan_architecture" class="mw-redirect" title="Tibetan architecture">Tibetan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Chinese_architecture" title="Chinese architecture">Chinese</a> as well as combinations of the three. Among the first quadratic temples was Batu-Tsagaan (1654) designed by <a href="/wiki/Zanabazar" title="Zanabazar">Zanabazar</a>. An example of the <i>ger</i>-style architecture is the lamasery Dashi-Choiling in Ulaanbaatar. The temple Lavrin (18th century) in the <a href="/wiki/Erdene_Zuu" class="mw-redirect" title="Erdene Zuu">Erdene Zuu</a> lamasery was built in the Tibetan tradition. An example of a temple built in the Chinese tradition is the lamasery Choijing Lamiin Sume (1904), which is a museum today. The quadratic temple Tsogchin in lamasery Gandan in Ulaanbaatar is a combination of the Mongolian and Chinese tradition. The temple of Maitreya (disassembled in 1938) is an example of the Tibeto-Mongolian architecture.<sup id="cite_ref-Chultem_155-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chultem-155">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> Dashi-Choiling monastery has commenced a project to restore the temple and the 25 metres (82&#160;ft) sculpture of Maitreya.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Mongolia" title="Music of Mongolia">Music of Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolian_Musician.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Mongolian_Musician.jpg/170px-Mongolian_Musician.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Mongolian_Musician.jpg/255px-Mongolian_Musician.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Mongolian_Musician.jpg/340px-Mongolian_Musician.jpg 2x" data-file-width="897" data-file-height="1196" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolian_Musician.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Musician playing the traditional Mongolian musical instrument <a href="/wiki/Morin_khuur" title="Morin khuur">morin khuur</a></div></div></div>
<p>The music of Mongolia is strongly influenced by nature, nomadism, shamanism, and also Tibetan Buddhism. The traditional music includes a variety of instruments, famously the <a href="/wiki/Morin_khuur" title="Morin khuur">morin khuur</a>, and also the singing styles like the <a href="/wiki/Long_song" title="Long song">urtyn duu</a> ("long song"), and throat-singing (<a href="/wiki/Tuvan_throat_singing#Khoomei" title="Tuvan throat singing">khoomei</a>). The "tsam" is danced to keep away evil spirits and it was seen as reminiscent of <a href="/wiki/Shamanism" title="Shamanism">shamanism</a>.
</p><p>The first rock band of Mongolia was <a href="/wiki/Soyol_Erdene" title="Soyol Erdene">Soyol Erdene</a>, founded in the 1960s. Their <a href="/wiki/The_Beatles" title="The Beatles">Beatles</a>-like manner was severely criticized by the communist censorship. It was followed by Mungunhurhree, Ineemseglel, Urgoo, etc., carving out the path for the genre in the harsh environment of communist ideology. Mungunhurhree and <a href="/wiki/Haranga" title="Haranga">Haranga</a> were to become the pioneers in the Mongolia's heavy rock music. Haranga approached its zenith in the late 1980s and 1990s.
</p><p>The leader of Haranga, famous guitarist Enh-Manlai, generously helped the growth of the following generations of rockers. Among the followers of Haranga was the band <a href="/wiki/Hurd_(band)" title="Hurd (band)">Hurd</a>. In the early 1990s, group Har-Chono pioneered Mongolia's folk-rock, merging elements of the Mongolian traditional "<a href="/wiki/Long_song" title="Long song">long song</a>" into the genre.
</p><p>By that time, the environment for development of artistic thought had become largely liberal thanks to the new democratic society in the country. The 1990s saw the development of rap, techno, hip-hop and also boy bands and girl bands flourished at the turn of the millennium.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Mongolia" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Mongolia">Media of Mongolia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolia_media.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Mongolia_media.jpg/220px-Mongolia_media.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Mongolia_media.jpg/330px-Mongolia_media.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Mongolia_media.jpg/440px-Mongolia_media.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1800" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mongolia_media.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mongolian media interviewing the opposition <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_Green_Party" title="Mongolian Green Party">Mongolian Green Party</a> in 2008. The media has gained significant freedoms since democratic reforms initiated in the 1990s.</div></div></div>
<p>Mongolian press began in 1920 with close ties to the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a> under the Mongolian Communist Party, with the establishment of the <i>Unen</i> ("Truth") newspaper similar to the Soviet <i><a href="/wiki/Pravda" title="Pravda">Pravda</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-pr_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pr-157">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> Until reforms in the 1990s, the government had strict control of the media and oversaw all publishing, in which no independent media were allowed.<sup id="cite_ref-pr_157-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pr-157">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Dissolution of the Soviet Union">dissolution of the Soviet Union</a> had a significant impact on Mongolia, where the <a href="/wiki/One-party_state" title="One-party state">one-party state</a> grew into a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party</a> democracy, and with that, media freedoms came to the forefront.
</p><p>A new law on press freedom, drafted with help from international NGOs on August 28, 1998 and enacted on January 1, 1999, paved the way for media reforms.<sup id="cite_ref-bruun_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bruun-158">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> The Mongolian media currently consists of around 300 print and broadcasting outlets.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-159">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 2006, the media environment has been improving with the government debating a new <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_information_laws_by_country" title="Freedom of information laws by country">Freedom of Information Act</a>, and the removal of any affiliation of media outlets with the government.<sup id="cite_ref-baner_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-baner-160">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> Market reforms have led to an annually increasing number of people working in the media, along with students at journalism schools.<sup id="cite_ref-baner_160-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-baner-160">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In its 2013 World Press Freedom Index report, <a href="/wiki/Reporters_Without_Borders" title="Reporters Without Borders">Reporters Without Borders</a> classified the media environment as 98th out of 179, with 1st being most free.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> In 2016, Mongolia was ranked 60th out of 180.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to 2014 Asian Development Bank survey, 80% of Mongolians cited television as their main source of information.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mongolian_cuisine">Mongolian cuisine</span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066933788"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_cuisine" title="Mongolian cuisine">Mongolian cuisine</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Mongolian_cuisine&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Dishes_of_Mongolian_cuisine.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Dishes_of_Mongolian_cuisine.jpg/220px-Dishes_of_Mongolian_cuisine.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Dishes_of_Mongolian_cuisine.jpg/330px-Dishes_of_Mongolian_cuisine.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Dishes_of_Mongolian_cuisine.jpg/440px-Dishes_of_Mongolian_cuisine.jpg 2x" data-file-width="810" data-file-height="1080" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Dishes_of_Mongolian_cuisine.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Various Mongolian meat dishes</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Boortsog_Aaruul.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Boortsog_Aaruul.JPG/220px-Boortsog_Aaruul.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Boortsog_Aaruul.JPG/330px-Boortsog_Aaruul.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Boortsog_Aaruul.JPG/440px-Boortsog_Aaruul.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3328" data-file-height="1872" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Boortsog_Aaruul.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>From smallest to largest: <a href="/wiki/Boortsog" title="Boortsog">boortsog</a> cookies, <a href="/wiki/Aaruul" class="mw-redirect" title="Aaruul">aaruul</a> (dried curds), and <a href="/wiki/Ul_boov" title="Ul boov">ul boov</a> cakes</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Mongolian_cuisine" title="Mongolian cuisine">Mongolian cuisine</a> predominantly consists of <a href="/wiki/Dairy_product" title="Dairy product">dairy products</a>, <a href="/wiki/Meat" title="Meat">meat</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Animal_fat" title="Animal fat">animal fats</a>. The most common rural dish is cooked <a href="/wiki/Lamb_and_mutton" title="Lamb and mutton">mutton</a>. In the city, steamed <a href="/wiki/Dumpling" title="Dumpling">dumplings</a> filled with meat—"<a href="/wiki/Buuz" title="Buuz">buuz</a>"— are popular.
</p>
The extreme <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Mongolia" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate of Mongolia">continental climate</a> of Mongolia has influenced the traditional diet. Use of <a href="/wiki/Vegetable" title="Vegetable">vegetables</a> and <a href="/wiki/Spice" title="Spice">spices</a> are limited. Due to geographic proximity and deep historic ties with <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a> and <a href="/wiki/Russia" title="Russia">Russia</a>, Mongolian cuisine is also influenced by <a href="/wiki/Chinese_cuisine" title="Chinese cuisine">Chinese</a> and <a href="/wiki/Russian_cuisine" title="Russian cuisine">Russian cuisine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Mongolian_cuisine_Marshall._268_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mongolian_cuisine_Marshall._268-165">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports_and_festivals">Sports and festivals</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mongolia_at_the_Olympics" title="Mongolia at the Olympics">Mongolia at the Olympics</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Naadamceremony2006.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Naadamceremony2006.jpg/220px-Naadamceremony2006.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Naadamceremony2006.jpg/330px-Naadamceremony2006.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Naadamceremony2006.jpg/440px-Naadamceremony2006.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Naadamceremony2006.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Naadam" title="Naadam">Naadam</a> is the largest summer celebration.</div></div></div>
<p>The main national festival is <a href="/wiki/Naadam" title="Naadam">Naadam</a>, which has been organised for centuries and takes place over three days in the summer, consisting of three Mongolian traditional sports, <a href="/wiki/Archery" title="Archery">archery</a>, cross-country horse-racing, and wrestling, traditionally recognized as the Three Manly Games of Naadam. In modern-day Mongolia, Naadam is held from July 11 to 13 in the honour of the anniversaries of the National Democratic Revolution and foundation of the Great Mongol State.
</p><p>Another very popular activity called Shagaa is the "flicking" of sheep ankle bones at a target several feet away, using a flicking motion of the finger to send the small bone flying at targets and trying to knock the target bones off the platform. At Naadam, this contest is popular among older Mongolians.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Three_Naadam_riders.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Three_Naadam_riders.jpg/220px-Three_Naadam_riders.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Three_Naadam_riders.jpg/330px-Three_Naadam_riders.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Three_Naadam_riders.jpg/440px-Three_Naadam_riders.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1616" data-file-height="1105" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Three_Naadam_riders.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Riders during <a href="/wiki/Naadam" title="Naadam">Naadam</a> festival</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Horse_culture_in_Mongolia" title="Horse culture in Mongolia">Horse riding</a> is especially central to Mongolian culture. The long-distance races that are showcased during Naadam festivals are one aspect of this, as is the popularity of trick riding. One example of trick riding is the legend that the Mongolian military hero <a href="/wiki/Damdin_S%C3%BCkhbaatar" title="Damdin Sükhbaatar">Damdin Sükhbaatar</a> scattered coins on the ground and then picked them up while riding a horse at full gallop.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mongolian_wrestling" title="Mongolian wrestling">Mongolian wrestling</a> is the most popular of all Mongol sports. It is the highlight of the Three Manly Games of Naadam. Historians claim that Mongol-style wrestling originated some seven thousand years ago. Hundreds of wrestlers from different cities and aimags around the country take part in the national wrestling competition.
</p><p>Other sports such as basketball, <a href="/wiki/Olympic_weightlifting" title="Olympic weightlifting">weightlifting</a>, <a href="/wiki/Powerlifting" title="Powerlifting">powerlifting</a>, association football, athletics, gymnastics, table tennis, <a href="/wiki/Jujutsu" title="Jujutsu">jujutsu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Karate" title="Karate">karate</a>, <a href="/wiki/Aikido" title="Aikido">aikido</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kickboxing" title="Kickboxing">kickboxing</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Mixed_martial_arts" title="Mixed martial arts">mixed martial arts</a> have become popular in Mongolia. More Mongolian table tennis players are competing internationally.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Freestyle_wrestling" title="Freestyle wrestling">Freestyle wrestling</a> has been practised since 1958 in Mongolia.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> Mongolian freestyle wrestlers have won the first and the most Olympic medals of Mongolia.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Naidangiin_T%C3%BCvshinbayar" title="Naidangiin Tüvshinbayar">Naidangiin Tüvshinbayar</a> won Mongolia's first ever Olympic gold medal in the men's 100-kilogram class of judo.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Amateur_boxing" title="Amateur boxing">Amateur boxing</a> has been practised in Mongolia since 1948.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> The Mongolian Olympic boxing national team was founded in 1960. The Communist government of Mongolia banned boxing from 1964 to 1967 but the government soon ended the ban. <a href="/wiki/Professional_boxing" title="Professional boxing">Professional boxing</a> began in Mongolia in the 1990s.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mongolia_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Mongolia national basketball team">Mongolia national basketball team</a> enjoyed some success recently, especially at the <a href="/wiki/Basketball_at_the_East_Asian_Games" title="Basketball at the East Asian Games">East Asian Games</a>.
</p><p>Association football is also played in Mongolia. The <a href="/wiki/Mongolia_national_football_team" title="Mongolia national football team">Mongolia national football team</a> began playing national games again during the 1990s; but has not yet qualified for a major international tournament. The <a href="/wiki/Mongolia_Premier_League" class="mw-redirect" title="Mongolia Premier League">Mongolia Premier League</a> is the top domestic competition.
</p><p>Several Mongolian women have excelled in <a href="/wiki/Shooting_sports" title="Shooting sports">pistol shooting</a>: <a href="/wiki/Otryadyn_G%C3%BCndegmaa" title="Otryadyn Gündegmaa">Otryadyn Gündegmaa</a> is a silver medalist of the 2008 Olympic Games, <a href="/wiki/Munkhbayar_Dorjsuren" title="Munkhbayar Dorjsuren">Munkhbayar Dorjsuren</a> is a double world champion and Olympic bronze medal winner (now representing Germany), while <a href="/wiki/Tsogbadrakhyn_M%C3%B6nkhzul" title="Tsogbadrakhyn Mönkhzul">Tsogbadrakhyn Mönkhzul</a> is, as of May 2007, ranked third in the world in the <a href="/w/index.php?title=25-metre_pistol&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="25-metre pistol (page does not exist)">25-metre pistol</a> event.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mongolian <a href="/wiki/Sumo" title="Sumo">sumo</a> wrestler <a href="/wiki/Dolgors%C3%BCrengiin_Dagvadorj" class="mw-redirect" title="Dolgorsürengiin Dagvadorj">Dolgorsürengiin Dagvadorj</a> won 25 top division tournament championships, placing him fourth on the <a href="/wiki/List_of_sumo_record_holders#Most_career_championships" title="List of sumo record holders">all-time list</a>. In January 2015, <a href="/wiki/M%C3%B6nkhbatyn_Davaajargal" class="mw-redirect" title="Mönkhbatyn Davaajargal">Mönkhbatyn Davaajargal</a> took his 33rd top division championship, giving him the most in the history of sumo.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Bandy" title="Bandy">Bandy</a> is the only sport in which Mongolia has finished higher than third place at the <a href="/wiki/Asian_Winter_Games" title="Asian Winter Games">Asian Winter Games</a>, which happened in <a href="/wiki/Bandy_at_the_2011_Asian_Winter_Games" title="Bandy at the 2011 Asian Winter Games">2011</a> when <a href="/wiki/Mongolia_national_bandy_team" title="Mongolia national bandy team">the national team</a> captured the silver medal. It led to being chosen as the best Mongolian sports team of 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Mongolia won the bronze medal of the B division at the <a href="/wiki/2017_Bandy_World_Championship" title="2017 Bandy World Championship">2017 Bandy World Championship</a> after which the then <a href="/wiki/President_of_Mongolia" title="President of Mongolia">President of Mongolia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tsakhiagiin_Elbegdorj" title="Tsakhiagiin Elbegdorj">Tsakhiagiin Elbegdorj</a>, held a reception for the team.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ulaanbataar holds an annual marathon in June. 2015 will have the sixth marathon that has been organized by Ar Mongol. The race starts at Sukh Bataar Square and is always open to residents and runners who come especially for this event.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Eagles_and_Hunters.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Eagles_and_Hunters.JPG/220px-Eagles_and_Hunters.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="91" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Eagles_and_Hunters.JPG/330px-Eagles_and_Hunters.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Eagles_and_Hunters.JPG/440px-Eagles_and_Hunters.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4270" data-file-height="1757" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Eagles_and_Hunters.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Kazakhs" title="Kazakhs">Kazakh</a> hunters in Mongolia with eagles</div></div></div>
<p>Mongolia holds other traditional festivals throughout the year. The <a href="/wiki/Golden_Eagle_Festival" title="Golden Eagle Festival">Golden Eagle Festival</a> draws about 400 eagle hunters on horseback, including the traveler <span title="Mongolian-language text"><span lang="mn-Cyrl">Мөнхбаярт Батсайхан</span></span> (<span title="Mongolian-language romanization"><i lang="mn-Latn">Mönkhbayart Batsaikhan</i></span>), to compete with their birds. The <a href="/wiki/Ice_Festival" class="mw-redirect" title="Ice Festival">Ice Festival</a> and the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Thousand_Camel_Festival&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Thousand Camel Festival (page does not exist)">Thousand Camel Festival</a> are amongst many other traditional Mongolian festivals.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-1">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Also spelled "Ulan Bator".</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-4"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-4">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">"Mongolian" denotes nationality, which includes minorities such as <a href="/wiki/Kazakhs" title="Kazakhs">Kazakhs</a> or <a href="/wiki/Tuvans" title="Tuvans">Tuvans</a>, while "Mongol" indicates <a href="/wiki/Mongols" title="Mongols">Mongol ethnicity</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-12"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-12">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Mongolian_language" title="Mongolian language">Mongolian</a>: <span lang="mn">Монгол Улс</span>, <small>romanized:&#160;</small><span title="Mongolian-language romanization"><i lang="mn-Latn">Mongol Uls</i></span>; <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_script" title="Mongolian script">Mongolian script</a>: <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1125315459"/><span class="font-mong" style="display:inline-block; font-weight:normal; font-size: 1.25em; line-height: 1.2em; -webkit-writing-mode: vertical-lr; -o-writing-mode: vertical-lr; -ms-writing-mode: tb-lr; writing-mode: tb-lr; writing-mode: vertical-lr; vertical-align:text-top;">ᠮᠤᠩᠭᠤᠯ</span> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1125315459"/><span class="font-mong" style="display:inline-block; font-weight:normal; font-size: 1.25em; line-height: 1.2em; -webkit-writing-mode: vertical-lr; -o-writing-mode: vertical-lr; -ms-writing-mode: tb-lr; writing-mode: tb-lr; writing-mode: vertical-lr; vertical-align:text-top;">ᠤᠯᠤᠰ</span>, <small>Mongolian pronunciation:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="mn-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Mongolian" title="Help:IPA/Mongolian">[mɔŋɢəɮ ʊɮs]</a></span>, <span title="Mongolian-script transliteration"><span>moŋɣol ulus</span></span>; <a href="/wiki/Literal_translation" title="Literal translation">lit.</a> "Mongol Nation" or "State of Mongolia"</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-34"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-34">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">This view is further explored in the school of thought of <a href="/wiki/New_Qing_History" title="New Qing History">New Qing History</a>.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<li id="cite_note-70"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-70">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.montsame.mn/en/read/219358">Official documents to be recorded in both scripts from 2025</a> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20200805003025/https://www.montsame.mn/en/read/219358">Archived</a> 5 August 2020 at the <a href="/wiki/Wayback_Machine" title="Wayback Machine">Wayback Machine</a>, Montsame, 18 March 2020.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-71"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-71">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://mongolia.gogo.mn/r/146942">Mongolian Language Law is effective from July 1st</a> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20220409222721/https://mongolia.gogo.mn/r/146942">Archived</a> 9 April 2022 at the <a href="/wiki/Wayback_Machine" title="Wayback Machine">Wayback Machine</a>, Gogo, 1 July 2015. "<b>Misinterpretation 1:</b> <i>Use of cyrillic is to be terminated and only Mongolian script to be used.</i> There is no provision in the law that states the termination of use of cyrillic. It clearly states that Mongolian script is to be added to the current use of cyrillic. Mongolian script will be introduced in stages and state and local government is to conduct their correspondence in both cyrillic and Mongolian script. This provision is to be effective starting January 1st of 2025. ID, birth certificate, marriage certificate and education certificates are to be both in Mongolian cyrillic and Mongolian script and currently Mongolian script is being used in official letters of President, Prime Minister and Speaker of Parliament."</span>
</li>

<li id="cite_note-Draft-79"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Draft_79-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFShugart2005" class="citation journal cs1"><a href="/wiki/Matthew_S%C3%B8berg_Shugart" title="Matthew Søberg Shugart">Shugart, Matthew Søberg</a> (September 2005). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20080819200307/http://dss.ucsd.edu/~mshugart/semi-presidentialism.pdf">"Semi-Presidential Systems: Dual Executive and Mixed Authority Patterns"</a> <span class="cs1-format">(PDF)</span>. <i>Graduate School of International Relations and Pacific Studies</i>. <li id="cite_note-Dual-80"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Dual_80-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFShugart2005" class="citation journal cs1"><a href="/wiki/Matthew_S%C3%B8berg_Shugart" title="Matthew Søberg Shugart">Shugart, Matthew Søberg</a> (December 2005). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.palgrave-journals.com/fp/journal/v3/n3/pdf/8200087a.pdf">"Semi-Presidential Systems: Dual Executive And Mixed Authority Patterns"</a> <span class="cs1-format">(PDF)</span>. <i>French Politics</i>. <b>3</b> (3): 323–351. <a href="/wiki/Doi_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="Doi (identifier)">doi</a>:<span class="cs1-lock-free" title="Freely accessible"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://doi.org/10.1057%2Fpalgrave.fp.8200087">10.1057/palgrave.fp.8200087</a></span>. <a href="/wiki/S2CID_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="S2CID (identifier)">S2CID</a>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://api.semanticscholar.org/CorpusID:73642272">73642272</a>. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20160304053112/http://www.palgrave-journals.com/fp/journal/v3/n3/pdf/8200087a.pdf">Archived</a> <span class="cs1-format">(PDF)</span> from the original on March 4, 2016<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">February 21,</span> 2016</span>. <q>Even if the president has no discretion in the forming of cabinets or the right to dissolve parliament, his or her constitutional authority can be regarded as 'quite considerable' in Duverger's sense if cabinet legislation approved in parliament can be blocked by the people's elected agent. Such powers are especially relevant if an extraordinary majority is required to override a veto, as in <b>Mongolia</b>, Poland, and Senegal.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=article&amp;rft.jtitle=French+Politics&amp;rft.atitle=Semi-Presidential+Systems%3A+Dual+Executive+And+Mixed+Authority+Patterns&amp;rft.volume=3&amp;rft.issue=3&amp;rft.pages=323-351&amp;rft.date=2005-12&amp;rft_id=info%3Adoi%2F10.1057%2Fpalgrave.fp.8200087&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fapi.semanticscholar.org%2FCorpusID%3A73642272%23id-name%3DS2CID&amp;rft.aulast=Shugart&amp;rft.aufirst=Matthew+S%C3%B8berg&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.palgrave-journals.com%2Ffp%2Fjournal%2Fv3%2Fn3%2Fpdf%2F8200087a.pdf&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AMongolia" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
